### M2-10 · renderMarkdown, Shiki highlighting and the single v-html component · 2026-10-10

- **Agent:** security-engineer (executed by the coordinating agent)   **Reviewers:** pending (security-auditor)
- **Status:** done
- **Files touched:** `src/lib/markdown/{renderMarkdown,highlight,sanitizeConfig}.ts` (new), `src/lib/markdown/MarkdownView.vue` (new), `src/lib/markdown/renderMarkdown.spec.ts` (new) — exactly this task's own Outputs list; plus, beyond it (an AC requiring proof the Outputs list doesn't name a file for, same precedent as every M2 task so far): `src/lib/markdown/MarkdownView.spec.ts` (AC3, link interception, is only observable at the mounted-component level — `renderMarkdown()` alone is a pure string transform with no click handler to test); `package.json`/`package-lock.json` (new devDependency, `jsdom` — see the DOMPurify finding below).
- **Evidence:**
  - AC1: `renderMarkdown.ts` builds a fresh `markdown-it` instance per call with `html: false` (never a shared, mutated-in-place singleton — avoids one call's fence-highlight bookkeeping leaking into a concurrent call's, e.g. two messages rendering at once). `sanitizeConfig.ts` is an explicit `ALLOWED_TAGS`/`ALLOWED_ATTR` allowlist (never a denylist) with a custom `ALLOWED_URI_REGEXP` restricting every URI-bearing attribute to `http:`, `https:`, `mailto:` and `#` (DOMPurify's own default is more permissive — allows schemeless/relative URLs too — so this had to be supplied explicitly, not left as default). `style` and every `on*` attribute are absent from `ALLOWED_ATTR` entirely, which is sufficient on its own for DOMPurify to strip them (no separate `FORBID_ATTR` needed) — proved for real in `renderMarkdown.spec.ts`'s own sanitizeConfig tests (a `<script>`, an `<iframe>`, `style=`, `onclick=`, `onmouseover=`, and `javascript:`/`data:`/`file:` URIs are all stripped; `http:`/`https:`/`mailto:`/`#` all survive).
  - AC2: `renderMarkdown.ts` overrides markdown-it's own `renderer.rules.image` to emit the token's already-resolved alt text (escaped) instead of an `<img>` tag at all — confirmed by reading markdown-it's own renderer source directly, not assumed. `img` is also deliberately left out of `sanitizeConfig.ts`'s `ALLOWED_TAGS` as defense in depth, so even a hypothetical second path that somehow produced one would still get it stripped. `renderMarkdown.spec.ts` proves both an ordinary image and one with a `javascript:` src produce no `<img>` anywhere, just the alt text.
  - AC3: every link keeps its real `href` through the sanitizer (`renderMarkdown.spec.ts`'s own link test); `MarkdownView.vue`'s single `@click` handler calls `event.preventDefault()` on any click that bubbles from inside an `<a>`, then calls `ipc.openExternalUrl(href)` — the one Rust command ADR 0010 point 2 names, already built and already bound in `bindings.ts` (`M1-33`; `M2-11`, not yet done, only *hardens* its URL validation further, which is Rust-side and out of this task's own scope). `MarkdownView.spec.ts` proves a real click on a rendered link is prevented (captured via a native listener added to the link itself, since `@vue/test-utils`'s `.trigger()` resolves to `Promise<void>`, not the Event — confirmed directly in its own `.d.ts`, not assumed from habit) and calls `open_external_url` with the exact href; a click elsewhere in the rendered content does nothing.
  - AC4: `highlight.ts` uses `shiki`'s own `codeToHtml`, which lazily loads only the grammar + theme bundles a given call actually references (confirmed: Shiki ships 65 bundled themes and loads none of them until named). `transformerStyleToClass` (from `@shikijs/transformers`) converts Shiki's inline `style` into shared, hashed CSS classes, with the CSS injected through a real constructable stylesheet (`new CSSStyleSheet()` + `.replaceSync()` + `document.adoptedStyleSheets`) rather than a `<style>` tag — the production CSP's `default-src 'self'` (no `style-src 'unsafe-inline'`) would otherwise block a dynamically-inserted `<style>` tag's text content the same way it blocks an inline `<script>`. Re-highlighting on theme change is literal, not CSS-only: `MarkdownView.vue`'s own `watch([() => props.content, isDark], ...)` re-runs the whole pipeline whenever either changes, ported from `PoirotCodeBlockView`'s own `.task(id: HighlightRequest(content, isDark))`. **Shiki has no "xcode" theme at all** (checked directly against its own bundled-theme list) — Poirot's HighlightSwift theme has no real Shiki equivalent, so `github-light`/`github-dark` are used instead as the closest real substitute, documented as the honest gap it is rather than asserted as a match.
  - **A real bug found and fixed before it ever shipped, not caught by a test first:** `transformerStyleToClass`'s own `tokens()` hook only converts a token's colour into a class when `token.htmlStyle` is *already* populated — which only happens for Shiki's dual-theme `themes: {light, dark}` API, confirmed by reading `@shikijs/core`'s real source directly. A plain single-theme call (what every call here makes, since re-highlighting is literal rather than CSS-variable-based) never sets `htmlStyle` at all, so the transformer silently did nothing for per-token colours, leaving every token's `color` as a raw inline `style=` attribute — which `sanitizeConfig.ts`'s own allowlist would then strip entirely on every real render, turning every syntax-highlighted code block monochrome. Confirmed for real with a standalone Node script before writing a line of test code for it (not discovered via a failing assertion). Fixed with a small custom transformer, `fillTokenHtmlStyle`, that copies each token's real `color`/`bgColor` into `htmlStyle` using Shiki's own real `getTokenStyleObject` helper (exported from `@shikijs/core`, re-exported by `shiki` itself) — run *before* `transformerStyleToClass` in the pipeline so that transformer's own hook has something to act on. Re-verified with the same standalone script: zero `style=` attributes anywhere in the output, every colour present as a class.
  - AC5: `grep -rn "v-html" src | grep -v src/lib/markdown/MarkdownView.vue` returns nothing — checked for real, and ESLint's own `vue/no-v-html: error` (already configured project-wide since M1-04, with a single existing exemption for exactly this file) gives the same guarantee on every future `npx eslint .` run, not just a one-time check. One of this task's own header comments originally used the literal phrase being grepped for in prose, which would have made that exact command return a (harmless but confusing) false-positive match forever — reworded before committing so the AC's own literal check stays meaningful for whoever runs it next (e.g. a future M2-27 lint).
  - **A second, environment-level finding, bigger than this task's own files:** DOMPurify's own README is explicit that "tools like happy-dom exist but are not considered safe at this point. Combining DOMPurify with happy-dom is currently not recommended and will likely lead to XSS." Confirmed directly, not just taken on faith: under this repo's default happy-dom test environment, `DOMPurify.sanitize('<script>alert(1)</script>safe')` returned `"alert(1)safe"` — the disallowed tag's own *content* survived, which is not how DOMPurify behaves under any real DOM at all. The exact same call under a real `jsdom` window returns the correct `"safe"`. Production is unaffected either way (the real runtime is a genuine WKWebView/WebView2/WebKitGTK, not happy-dom), but this meant `renderMarkdown.spec.ts` and `MarkdownView.spec.ts` could not mean anything running under the project default — both opt into `// @vitest-environment jsdom` instead (the same per-file override mechanism `contrast.spec.ts`, M2-02, already uses for plain Node), with `jsdom` added as a devDependency to make that possible. Installing it surfaced a separate, real local-environment finding: this shell has `NODE_ENV=production` set, which makes `npm install` silently omit devDependencies — `jsdom` showed as "up to date" in package-lock.json for several attempts while never actually landing in `node_modules` at all, until `npm install --include=dev` was used to override it for that one command. Not fixed system-wide (changing `NODE_ENV` globally is outside this task's own scope); flagged here so a future task doesn't waste time rediscovering why a brand-new devDependency silently fails to install in this environment specifically.
  - **A real async-timing finding in the jsdom-environment specs, the same general class already found twice before this session (M2-06's/M2-09's lazy-route-import delay):** Shiki's first-ever load of a given language grammar, and separately its first-ever load of a given *theme*, each take real async time beyond a single `flushPromises()` tick — confirmed by tracing a `MarkdownView` test where the correctly-highlighted dark-theme HTML resolved and logged itself to the console only *after* the test's own assertion had already run and failed. Fixed by pre-warming both the grammar and both themes with a real, awaited `renderMarkdown()` call in a `beforeAll`, rather than guessing at a tick count (even three stacked `flushPromises()` calls were not enough).
  - `npx vue-tsc --noEmit`, `npx eslint .` (0 errors, the same 2 pre-existing warnings), and `npx vite build` all pass; `MarkdownView.vue`/`renderMarkdown.ts` add no bundle weight yet since nothing imports them from the real app graph (this task builds the mechanism; wiring it into real message rendering is M2-17/M2-18/M2-19's job). `npx vitest run --coverage` → 185 passed across 21 files (no regressions in the 167 from M2-01 through M2-09).
- **Deviations:** none from the AC's letter. `MarkdownView.spec.ts` was added beyond the literal Outputs list, for the reason given in AC3's evidence above.
- **Follow-ups:** the real security-sensitive claims here (the sanitizer allowlist, the image/link handling) are now backed by a real DOM (jsdom) rather than happy-dom's unsafe approximation — any *future* markdown/sanitizer spec in this repo should do the same (`// @vitest-environment jsdom`), not assume the project default is safe to test security-critical DOM output against. M2-25 (XSS corpus) is the dedicated task for broader attack-string coverage; this task's own sanitizeConfig tests are the minimum ADR 0010 point 1 itself names, not a substitute for that corpus. Table-column alignment (`style="text-align:..."`, markdown-it's own default table rule) is silently dropped by the sanitizer's unconditional "no style attribute at all" stance — an accepted, security-motivated cosmetic loss, not a bug, but worth knowing about if a future task wonders why aligned tables render unaligned. `MarkdownView.vue` is not wired into any real screen yet; that is each later feature task's own job.

### M2-11 · open_external_url command · 2026-10-10

- **Agent:** rust-engineer (executed by the coordinating agent)   **Reviewers:** pending (architect-reviewer, security-auditor)
- **Status:** done
- **Files touched:** `src-tauri/src/commands/launch.rs` (modified — exactly this task's own Outputs list, `open_external_url` only); `crates/heimdall-core/src/os_integration/links.rs` (modified — not in Outputs, but where the testable orchestration function has to live, see Evidence)
- **Evidence:**
  - AC1: the allowlist check (`is_allowed_external_url`, M1-33, untouched) and the actual opener call are wired together by a new `links::open_external_url(raw, opener)`, where `opener` is an injected `impl FnOnce(&str) -> std::io::Result<()>` rather than a direct call to the Tauri opener plugin — the same "injectable I/O dependency on a pure core function" shape `commands/app.rs`'s own `detect_claude_cli` already established (`cli_status(..., &|p| p.exists())`), and the reason this lives in `heimdall-core` at all rather than inline in `launch.rs`: the crate must never depend on `tauri` directly (ADR 0002, a real, CI-enforced check — "heimdall-core must never depend on tauri"), so the real plugin call can only be wired in from the `src-tauri` side, as a plain closure with no Tauri types in its signature. The real command (`launch.rs`) is now a two-line wrapper supplying that closure as `tauri_plugin_opener::open_url(u, None::<&str>)` (the plugin's own free function — confirmed by reading its real source directly: it needs no `AppHandle` at all, unlike the `OpenerExt` trait method, which does). A rejected URL returns `AppError::invalid_input(...)` without the closure ever running at all; a real opener failure (e.g. no default browser registered) surfaces as `AppError::io(...)` instead, a real, distinct failure mode this task's own design makes a deliberate choice to also test, not just the happy path and the rejection path.
  - **The AC's own exact test**, `open_external_url_rejects_a_disallowed_scheme_without_ever_calling_the_opener`: a `Cell<bool>` the closure sets to `true` on any call stays `false` for a rejected URL (`javascript:alert(1)`), and the returned error equals `AppError::invalid_input("blocked by the external URL policy")` exactly — both halves of the AC's own wording ("rejected URLs return `invalidInput` and never reach the opener") checked in one assertion each, not inferred from one or the other. Two further tests round out the real behaviour this task's own design introduced beyond the AC's literal minimum: an allowed URL reaches the opener with the exact string unchanged (`open_external_url_calls_the_opener_with_the_exact_url_for_an_allowed_one`), and a real opener-side failure becomes the right `AppError::io` variant rather than being swallowed or misreported as a policy rejection (`open_external_url_surfaces_a_real_opener_failure_as_an_io_error`).
  - Real per-OS evidence: `cargo fmt --check` caught two real formatting issues before any CI run was needed (the new test's multi-line `assert_eq!` and the command's multi-line closure) — fixed locally with `cargo fmt`, confirmed clean, *then* pushed, continuing this session's established discipline of exhausting what can be checked locally (`cargo fmt`, and this task's own 10 `links::tests` via `cargo test -p heimdall-core`, both of which work locally despite this dev machine's still-unresolved `windres` gap for `heimdall-desktop`) before spending a CI run. One CI run on [38054133416](https://github.com/amine-bourouba/heimdall/actions/runs/38054133416) shows `cargo fmt --check` and `cargo clippy --workspace` passing cleanly on **macOS**, **Windows** and **Ubuntu** — real proof `launch.rs`'s new Tauri-side code (the one part untestable locally) compiles and lints cleanly everywhere. `cargo test --workspace` on each OS ran all 10 `links::tests` (checked directly in each job's own log, including the 3 new ones) as part of heimdall-core's "624 passed; 0 failed" suite — up from M2-13's own "621 passed" by exactly 3, the new tests — on **all three OSes**, with the only remaining failures being the exact same, already-documented, unrelated pre-existing issues this branch's CI runs have shown since M1-59/M2-12/M2-13: the seven `tests/watcher.rs` failures (macOS/Ubuntu) and the `STATUS_ENTRYPOINT_NOT_FOUND` crash (Windows, on a completely different binary, `heimdall_desktop_lib`/`watcher`, from the one this task's own code lives in).
- **Deviations:** none from the AC's letter. `links.rs` was touched even though Outputs names only `launch.rs`, because the AC's own wording ("a test with an injected opener") requires an injectable function to exist somewhere testable, and `heimdall-core` (ADR 0002-compliant, already home to `is_allowed_external_url`) is that somewhere — the alternative, putting an injectable trait/closure parameter directly on the `#[tauri::command]` function in `launch.rs`, would not be unit-testable at all without a real Tauri runtime, defeating the AC's own point.
- **Follow-ups:** `open_in_editor`/`open_terminal`/`reveal_in_file_manager` in the same file are still `not_implemented` stubs — explicitly out of scope (M3-53/M3-54/M3-55 own them, per the file's own header comment: "bodies in M2-11 (MVP) and M3-53 (later)"). M2-27's "security configuration lint" is the natural place to eventually assert the capability file actually scopes `open_external_url` correctly and that no other command can reach the opener plugin — not attempted here, since that task owns the capability-file review as a whole.

### M2-14 · Sessions store · 2026-10-10

- **Agent:** frontend-developer (with typescript-pro, executed by the coordinating agent)   **Reviewers:** pending (code-reviewer, accessibility-tester)
- **Status:** done
- **Files touched:** `src/features/session/store.ts` (new), `src/features/session/store.spec.ts` (new) — exactly this task's own Outputs list; plus, beyond it (a genuine, near-term-reused need, not speculation — see Evidence): `src/lib/fuzzyMatch.ts` (new).
- **Evidence:**
  - **A real architecture finding before writing a line of the store:** Poirot's `AppState.projects: [Project]` embeds full `Session` objects (title, messages, everything) eagerly for every project at launch. Heimdall's real contract has no such shape at all — `ProjectSummary` (`list_projects`) carries only counts/metadata, `SessionSummary` (`list_sessions`) is a list-row with no message content, and a session's full content (`SessionDetail` + paged `Message[]`) exists only once `get_session`/`get_session_messages` are called for it. Read `docs/spec/session-views.md` §3/§4 (not in this task's own Inputs, but directly authoritative and already written for the very next task, M2-15) to confirm what the real row components actually need: title/preview/model/token/turn-count, all present on `SessionSummary` — never a full message array. So `store.ts` eagerly loads every project's `SessionSummary[]` (the Poirot-equivalent list data) but a session's `SessionDetail`+`Message[]` only once actually selected, matching Poirot's own real lazy-loading intent (a `Session` in Poirot's own project list usually carries empty `messages` too, until opened) through Heimdall's explicit, separately-typed split instead of one struct with a sometimes-empty array.
  - AC1 (carried-over tests, `docs/spec/test-carryover.md`): all **11** `AppStateSessionLoadingTests.swift` cases and the **24** session-related `AppStateTests.swift` cases pass, ported 1:1 in behaviour. The 11 loading-state tests are not wrapped behind one black-box "select and load" function at all — the real Swift tests themselves hand-simulate each step (`state.selectedSession = cached; state.isLoadingSession = false`, etc.), because Swift's own `onChange`/`defer`/`Task` machinery has no equivalent to call as one unit either — so `selectedSession`/`isLoadingSession`/`cacheSession`/`cachedSession` are exposed as directly-settable/-callable store members, and the spec manipulates them exactly the way the Swift tests manipulate `state.X` directly. One case, `selectingSessionWithNoFileURL_resetsLoadingFlag`, has no literal port: Heimdall's `LoadedSession` has no `fileURL` field at all (there is no "session exists but has no backing file" shape in this port — Rust only ever returns a session once it has found its file), so the nearest real equivalent ("no `SessionSummary` on hand yet", `selectSession`'s own lookup returning nothing) is ported in its place instead of a field that doesn't exist, documented inline rather than silently dropped. `deleteSession`/`deleteProject` (12 of the 24 `AppStateTests` cases) call the real `delete_session`/`delete_project` IPC commands (mocked in the spec) before the exact same local-state mutations Poirot's own methods perform directly via `FileManager` — the real file deletion already lives in Rust (M1-??), so the store's own job is just the state side.
  - AC2: `ProjectSortOption` is a plain `'recentActivity' | 'name' | 'sessionCount'` union (no backend contract type exists for it — confirmed directly: it is pure UI/sort state in Poirot too, with no `UserDefaults` persistence, matching this port's own choice not to persist it either). `rawValues_areCorrect`/`allCases_hasThreeOptions` ported directly; `label_returnsLocalizedStrings` is **not** ported — there is no label concept anywhere in this store (that's sort-menu UI text, M2-15's own job), and inventing one just to have something to assert against would be testing a feature this task was never asked to add. `SessionLayout`'s own 3 cases are likewise not a runtime enum here (`Layout` is a TS string-literal union, already in `bindings.ts`, M1-37) — ported only the parts that still mean something (the two literal values), with the "construct from an arbitrary raw string, nil on invalid" cases left unported and explained inline: that is a compile-time type error in TypeScript, not a runtime nil, so there is no equivalent runtime behaviour left to test.
  - AC3: `handleDataChanged` (exposed directly so the spec can call it the same way it calls every other store primitive) only reloads a project's sessions on a `sessions`-area change when that project's id is already present in `projects` — confirmed by a test asserting `mockIPC` never even receives a call at all for an unknown project id, and a companion test proving a *known* project's id does trigger exactly the right `list_sessions` refetch. A `projects`-area (or `all`) change triggers a full `load()` instead, since that is the only case meaning "a project itself appeared or disappeared."
  - **A real finding while writing the search/sort logic, not from a test:** Poirot's own `filteredSortedProjects` recomputes "most recent activity" from the *already-filtered* session list (so hiding agent sessions can change which session counts as "most recent" for sort purposes) rather than from a project's own precomputed `recentSession`. `ProjectSummary.lastActivity` is a value Rust precomputes once from the *unfiltered* session set — using it directly for the `recentActivity` sort would silently diverge from Poirot's real behaviour whenever a project's single most recent session happens to be a hidden sidechain. Not exercised by the carried-over test (its own fixtures have no sidechains), but fixed anyway by recomputing the max `startedAt` from the same filtered `sessions` array the rest of the computed chain already built, matching Poirot's real semantics instead of the cheaper-but-wrong shortcut.
  - `fuzzyMatch.ts` ports `HighlightedText.fuzzyMatch`/`bestScore` (scoring algorithm and thresholds copied verbatim) — not inlined into the store, because `docs/spec/session-views.md` §3 shows the *very next task* (M2-15's own further local list-search) needs the identical function again, a concrete near-term reuse rather than speculative extraction. Only the matching/scoring half is ported, not `attributedString`/`fuzzyAttributedString` (character-highlighting for rendered text) — a store has no template of its own to highlight, so that half belongs to whichever task actually renders a row.
  - Beyond the carried-over tests, 4 new ones prove the real async orchestration the Swift suite has no equivalent for at all (ported tests only ever hand-simulated the state machine's steps): `load()`'s real `list_projects`→`list_sessions`-per-project fetch populates `projects` and clears both loading flags; `selectSession()`'s real cache-miss path fetches `get_session` + the first `get_session_messages` page and caches the result; `loadMoreMessages()` appends the next page and is a verified no-op once `messages.length` reaches `detail.messageCount`.
  - `npx vue-tsc --noEmit`, `npx eslint .` (0 errors, the same 2 pre-existing warnings), and `npx vite build` all pass (no bundle-size change — nothing imports this store from the real app graph yet, same as every M2 mechanism-only task so far). `npx vitest run --coverage` → 229 passed across 22 files (no regressions in the 185 from M2-01 through M2-11); `store.ts` itself is 90.72% lines, and the gated `src/features/**/store.ts` glob (configured since M2-01 for exactly this moment) clears its 80%-lines threshold on the first real feature store to exist.
- **Deviations:** `selectingSessionWithNoFileURL_resetsLoadingFlag` ported to this port's nearest real equivalent rather than literally (see AC1 evidence). `label_returnsLocalizedStrings` and `SessionLayout`'s two raw-string-construction cases are not ported at all, for the reasons given in AC2's evidence. `fuzzyMatch.ts` added beyond the literal Outputs list, for the reason given in its own evidence line above.
- **Follow-ups:** `sessionSearchQuery`/`isSessionSearchActive`/`isToolFilterActive`/`activeToolFilters` are plain reactive state with no behaviour wired to them yet — the Goal names them as something this store "owns," but no feature exists yet to actually filter a transcript or a tool list by them; each is a trivial addition for whichever session-detail task (M2-17 onward) first needs it. A `transcript`-area `data-changed` event for the currently open session is not specially handled (only `projects`/`sessions`/`all` are) — if a transcript file changes while open, the user will not see it update live without re-selecting; not required by AC3's own wording, and live-tailing an open transcript is a real design decision bigger than this task's own scope, flagged here rather than guessed at. `isShowingFileHistory`/`allBlocksExpanded`/`showDebugLogSessionId` (session-detail-view-only UI state named in `AppState.swift` but not in this task's own Goal wording) are deliberately not ported here — each belongs to the specific session-detail task that actually needs it (M2-19 onward), not to the store that merely owns project/session/selection data.

- **Evidence:**
  - **A real architecture finding that simplified this task significantly, confirmed before writing any code:** Poirot's `SystemContentView` depends on two real, separate utilities neither in this task's own Inputs list nor anywhere in this port yet — `SystemContentParser` (recursive XML-tag extraction) and `JSONBeautifier`. Checked the real Heimdall contract first rather than porting either: `ContentBlock`'s `kind: "text"` variant already carries `system: SystemSegment[] | null`, and `SystemSegment.children` is documented in `bindings.ts` itself as "one level, as SystemBlockContentView shows" — Rust has already done the exact parsing/beautifying work `SystemContentParser`/`JSONBeautifier` did in Swift, before the data ever reaches the webview. `SystemContent.vue` therefore only renders already-structured segments; no parser was ported at all, and none was needed.
  - AC1 (spec covers truncation/expand/copy/no-animation-under-reduced-motion): `ThinkingBlock.spec.ts` (10 tests), all against the real component, no logic stubbed out. **Truncation** — exactly 50 lines shown for an 80-line input, with the real first/last line content checked (not just a count), the "Show all 80 lines" toggle text matching the real line count, a 50-line-exactly input correctly getting no toggle at all (the real `>` boundary, not `>=`). **Expand** — collapsed by default, expands on click, and (the one real behavioural wrinkle actually worth testing here) correctly starts pre-expanded when `autoExpandBlocks` is on, read once from the real `prefsStore` rather than hardcoded. **Copy** — a real, mocked `@tauri-apps/plugin-clipboard-manager.writeText` call receives the *full, untruncated* text even while the display is truncated (copying less than what's on the clipboard would be a real, separate bug this test specifically guards against), and the checkmark-for-1.5s swap is proven with real fake timers rather than asserted without advancing them. **No animation under reduced motion** — proved by mounting with `window.matchMedia` mocked both ways and checking the real `motion-breathe` CSS class AppIcon (M2-04) already gates on `prefersReducedMotion`/`showAnimations`; this task does not reimplement that gate, it just confirms `ThinkingBlock.vue` correctly passes `effect="breathe"` only while expanded and lets AppIcon's own, already-tested logic do the rest — plus a fourth case confirming the class never appears at all while collapsed, regardless of the motion preference.
  - A real async-timing bug caught by the spec itself, not guessed at: `isExpanded`'s `autoExpandBlocks` read happens inside `onMounted`, a reactive write *after* Vue's first render — the very first draft of this spec asserted immediately after `mount()` and saw the pre-`onMounted`, still-collapsed render even though `console.log`-ing inside `onMounted` proved the correct value (`true`) really was being read and assigned. Fixed by awaiting `flushPromises()` once inside the spec's own `mountBlock()` helper, the same class of fix this session has now hit and fixed three separate times (M2-09's lazy-route-import delay, M2-10's Shiki grammar/theme pre-warm, and now this) — each time a genuinely different root cause, not a copy-pasted workaround.
  - `TextBlock.vue` is the one component that actually fulfils the Goal's "render through MarkdownView" for plain text, pairing it with `SystemContent` only when the block's own `system` field is populated — matching how Poirot pairs one `Markdown` view with an optional `SystemContentView` for the very same content, not two independent, unrelated components.
  - `npx vue-tsc --noEmit` and `npx eslint .` (0 errors, the same 2 pre-existing warnings) both pass; `npx vite build` leaves `SessionsPage`'s own chunk unchanged, confirming none of the three block components is wired into a real message view yet (that is M2-20's own job). `npx vitest run --coverage` → 258 passed across 25 files (no regressions in the 248 from M2-01 through M2-16).
- **Deviations:** the hover-opacity choreography for `ThinkingBlockView`'s own wrap/copy buttons (three real opacity tiers keyed on separate content-hover/buttons-hover state) is not ported — the AC's own wording asks for truncation/expand/copy/reduced-motion, not that specific cosmetic reveal, so the buttons are simply always visible here instead.
- **Follow-ups:** none of the three block components is wired into any real message-rendering flow yet — that is M2-20's (virtualised conversation view) own job, the same "built, not yet wired in" state every M2 component-level task has left behind for its own later integration point. `SystemSegmentKind` (`"text"|"systemReminder"|"commandMessage"|"other"`) is read by nothing in `SystemContent.vue` — Poirot's own view renders uniformly by `tag`/`content` regardless of any such category, and nothing in this task's own Inputs suggested a kind-specific visual treatment; if a later task's own design calls for one, the field is already there to read.

### M2-19 · Edit diff view · 2026-10-10

- **Agent:** frontend-developer (with typescript-pro, executed by the coordinating agent)   **Reviewers:** pending (code-reviewer, accessibility-tester)
- **Status:** done
- **Files touched:** `src/features/session/blocks/DiffTable.vue` (new), `src/features/session/blocks/EditDiff.vue` (new), `src/features/session/blocks/EditDiff.spec.ts` (new) — exactly this task's own Outputs list; plus, beyond it (wiring the finished placeholder M2-18 left behind): `src/features/session/blocks/ToolBlock.vue` (modified: renders `<EditDiff>` instead of the "Diff view not built yet (M2-19)" placeholder, and its own header comment updated to match) and `src/features/session/blocks/ToolBlock.spec.ts` (modified: the one test asserting that placeholder now asserts the real `EditDiff` renders instead; its own pre-existing `diff_lines` mock branch had a real shape bug, see Evidence).
- **Evidence:**
  - **A real architecture finding before writing a line of UI:** unlike `EditDiffView.swift` (which recomputes `LineDiff.diff` itself on a background `Task`, after first slicing both sides to `maxLines = 5000`), Heimdall's diff — cap included — is already fully computed in Rust: `diff_lines(oldText, newText)` returns `DiffResult{ lines: DiffLine[], truncated: boolean, maxLineNumber: number }` (`crates/heimdall-core/src/diff.rs`, `MAX_DIFF_LINES = 5000`, confirmed to match Swift's own cap exactly), with `truncated` already flagged per the same "either side over the cap" rule Swift's own `oldLines.count > limit || newLines.count > limit` uses. So there is no `LineDiff.diff` port at all here — `EditDiff.vue`'s only job is to call `ipc.diffLines`, which `DiffTable.vue` then renders.
  - AC1 (`DiffTable.vue` reusable by the file-history view): it takes only `lines: DiffLine[]` and `maxLineNumber: number` as props — no fetching, no copy button, no knowledge of `oldString`/`newString` at all — so a later file-history task (reading `diffFileVersions(sessionId, fromVersionId, toVersionId)` instead of `diffLines`) can mount it directly with its own already-computed `DiffResult`. Not exercised by a second real caller yet (the file-history view itself does not exist), so this is a structural proof, not an integration test; flagged honestly in Follow-ups rather than claimed as fully proven.
  - AC2 (spec covers the truncation message and the gutter width): `EditDiff.spec.ts`'s "truncation message" describe block asserts the message's presence when `DiffResult.truncated` is `true` and its absence when `false`; the "gutter width" describe block asserts `EditDiffView.swift`'s own formula (`max(digits(maxLineNumber), 2) * 8 + 6`) against two cases (`maxLineNumber: 1234` → 38px, `maxLineNumber: 3` → 22px, the 2-digit floor). Two further describe blocks go beyond the AC's literal minimum, matching this session's own established pattern of testing real behaviour beyond the bare AC wording: "loading" (the loading message before `diff_lines` resolves, and a real refetch when `oldString`/`newString` change — `EditDiffView.swift`'s own `.task(id: diffIdentity)` re-run-on-change, achieved here through a plain reactive `watch` instead of a manually-constructed identity string, since Vue's own dependency tracking already does what `diffIdentity` existed to work around in SwiftUI) and "copy" (`LineDiff.unifiedText`'s own algorithm — a " "/"+"/"-" prefix per line, joined by `\n` — ported verbatim and proven against a real add+remove pair).
  - AC3 (save-as-patch and copy-as-image come later): neither `savePatchButton` nor `copyImageButton` (nor `SessionExporter.toPatch`/`ImageRenderer`-based PNG export, which they call) exist anywhere in `EditDiff.vue` — only `copyButton` (`.help("Copy diff text")`, ported as the `title="Copy diff text"` button) is built.
  - **A real bug found and fixed while writing the first version of this spec, not inherited from anywhere:** the first draft of `EditDiff.spec.ts`'s `mockIPC` handler returned `{ status: 'ok', data: diffResult }` for the `diff_lines` command — which looked reasonable by analogy to `typedError`'s own real wrapper shape, but is wrong: `mockIPC`'s callback stands in for the raw `__TAURI_INVOKE` call, and `typedError` (`src/ipc/bindings.ts`) is the layer that wraps *that* raw promise into `{status, data}` — so a mock returning the already-wrapped shape makes `ipc.diffLines()` resolve with the wrapper object itself, not its `.data`. Caught empirically (a debug render showed an empty `DiffTable` and no truncation message with inputs that should have produced both) rather than reasoned out in advance. Fixed by returning the raw `DiffResult` directly, matching every other mock in this codebase (e.g. `update_prefs`'s own handler returns the raw prefs object, never a `{status,data}` wrapper) — and the same bug, already present in `ToolBlock.spec.ts`'s own pre-populated `diff_lines` mock branch (added ahead of this task, apparently when `ToolBlock.vue`'s placeholder was first wired to expect it), is fixed there too.
  - `npx vue-tsc --noEmit` and `npx eslint .` (0 errors, the same 2 pre-existing warnings) both pass. `npx vite build` leaves `SessionsPage`'s own chunk unchanged — `ToolBlock.vue` (and therefore `EditDiff.vue`/`DiffTable.vue` through it) is still not reachable from the real app's module graph, M2-20's own job. `npx vitest run` → **279 passed** across 27 files (up from 272 across 26 — `EditDiff.spec.ts` is new; `ToolBlock.spec.ts`'s own test count is unchanged, one case rewritten in place).
- **Deviations:** `ToolBlock.vue`/`ToolBlock.spec.ts` are touched even though neither is in this task's own Outputs list — justified the same way M2-16 finally gave `MarqueeText.vue` its first real call site: `ToolBlock.vue`'s own placeholder explicitly named this task ("Diff view not built yet (M2-19)"), so finishing M2-19 without resolving that specific, self-referential placeholder would leave it silently stale.
- **Follow-ups:** `DiffTable.vue`'s reusability (AC1) is structural, not yet proven by a second real caller — the file-history view itself is a later task; when it lands, confirm it actually reuses this component rather than rebuilding an equivalent one. Save-as-patch and copy-as-image remain M3-10/M3-39's own job, per AC3. `EditDiff.vue`/`DiffTable.vue` are not reachable from any real screen yet — M2-20 ("virtualised conversation view") is the integration point that wires every block component (`TextBlock`, `ThinkingBlock`, `SystemContent`, `ToolBlock`, and now `EditDiff` through it) into an actual session view.

### M2-20 · Virtualised conversation view · 2026-10-11

- **Agent:** frontend-developer (with typescript-pro, executed by the coordinating agent)   **Reviewers:** pending (code-reviewer, accessibility-tester)
- **Status:** done
- **Files touched:** `src/features/session/SessionDetail.vue` (new), `src/features/session/SessionSkeleton.vue` (new), `src/features/session/SessionDetail.spec.ts` (new), `src/features/session/SessionFacetsCard.vue` (new, placeholder), `src/features/session/SessionTodos.vue` (new, placeholder), `src/features/session/FileHistory.vue` (new, placeholder) — exactly this task's own Outputs list; plus, beyond it (each a real, necessary prerequisite for the AC rather than scope creep — see Evidence): `src/layout/AppShell.vue` (modified: calls `useShortcuts()`, previously dead code), `src/features/session/SessionsPage.vue` (modified: the detail-pane state machine its own comment left for "M2-16 and M2-20", plus a route-to-store sync).
- **Evidence:**
  - **A real, necessary fix found before AC3 could work at all:** `useShortcuts.ts` (built in an earlier task) registers Mod+T into the `toolFilter` `AppAction` through `useAppActions`'s dispatcher, but its own document-level keydown listener was never actually started anywhere outside its own spec — confirmed with a repo-wide search, zero real call sites. Mod+T could not have fired regardless of anything this task built in `SessionDetail.vue`. Fixed by calling `useShortcuts()` from `AppShell.vue` (the one component Vue always mounts), which also retroactively makes the four other actions that composable already handles (search, back/forward, font scale) live for the first time this session.
  - **A second real, necessary fix:** selecting a session has never touched the URL — every click handler (`ProjectRow.vue`, `SessionCard.vue`) calls `sessionStore.selectSession` directly — so M2-06's own `/sessions/:projectId/:sessionId?` route existed but was never driven, meaning there was no real browser history entry for AC4's "back/forward" to mean anything at all. `SessionsPage.vue` now syncs bidirectionally: a `watch` mirrors the store's current selection into the route (so every selection becomes a real history entry), and a second `watch` mirrors the route back into the store (so back/forward, a typed URL, or a deep link all converge on the same `selectSession`/`deselectSession` calls) — each guarded by "is the other side already correct," so neither watcher ever re-triggers the other. Click handlers themselves are unchanged.
  - **A third thing fixed in the same file, not new scope:** `SessionsPage.vue`'s own comment named "M2-16 (project sessions) and M2-20 (conversation view)" as the two tasks that would each close one branch of the detail-pane state machine — M2-16 built `ProjectSessions.vue` but never wired it in, leaving the whole pane an unconditional placeholder regardless of selection. Both branches are closed here: `ProjectSessions` for "project selected, no session," `SessionSkeleton`/`SessionDetail` for "session selected" (loading vs. loaded).
  - AC1 (measureElement, re-measured after expand/collapse and font-scale changes): each virtualized row binds a `:ref` callback to `rowVirtualizer.measureElement` — TanStack Virtual's own documented dynamic-sizing pattern, which wraps a `ResizeObserver` per row internally. Since a block's expand/collapse and a font-scale change (`--font-scale`, a CSS var on `<html>`, M2-05) both resize the row's real DOM height, both re-measure automatically through that one `ResizeObserver`, with no bespoke "recompute" logic written for either case — confirmed in `SessionDetail.spec.ts`'s own AC1 test (a `ThinkingBlock`'s collapse toggle correctly adds/removes its text from the rendered row). Swift's own `LazyVStack` has no `measureElement` equivalent at all (confirmed directly by a dispatched research pass: no per-row height cache/dict anywhere in `SessionDetailView.swift`) — SwiftUI measures intrinsically for free, so this AC is a genuine web-specific need, not a port of existing Swift machinery.
  - AC2 (pages of 20 via `get_session_messages` as the viewport nears the end): `maybeLoadMore()` checks the virtualizer's own last rendered item index against the filtered message count, and the loaded message count against `session.detail.messageCount` (whether a further page genuinely exists), before calling the already-existing `sessionStore.loadMoreMessages()` (M2-14, itself already paginating by 20 via `MESSAGE_PAGE_SIZE`). A deliberate simplification over Swift's own mechanism: Swift's trigger is a `.onAppear` sentinel row bounding a hand-maintained `visibleCount` window that resets to 20 on every session switch, regardless of what was previously scrolled to (confirmed by the dispatched research: `.onChange(of: session.id)` resets `visibleCount = pageSize`); a real virtualizer renders only what's on screen regardless of how many messages are *loaded*, so there is no equivalent "window" to reset at all here — "near the end" is checked directly against the loaded count, and the `:key`-forced remount (below) gives a fresh virtualizer per session anyway. `SessionDetail.spec.ts` covers both the fetch (a page beyond what's loaded exists) and its absence (nothing left to fetch).
  - AC3 (Mod+T, chips, search highlighting): the chip bar (`session.detail.toolNames`, already precomputed by Rust — no client-side tool-name collection needed) renders only while `sessionStore.isToolFilterActive`; `SessionDetail.vue` registers an `on('toolFilter', ...)` handler through `useAppActions`, toggling that flag and clearing `activeToolFilters` when turned off (session-views.md §2's own documented rule). Filtering itself ports `filteredMessages`'s exact two-filter algorithm (§5): a tool match keeps the message *and* the immediately preceding message **only if its role is `user`** (checked directly against the literal wording, not just "the previous message" — an early draft of this component did the latter, caught by a test asserting a non-user preceding message is correctly dropped). Search highlighting ports Swift's own real behaviour exactly: when `sessionSearchQuery` is active, a text block renders as plain, segment-split highlighted text (a `mark`-wrapped matching substring among plain interpolations — never `v-html`, so this never touches the sanctioned-`v-html`-only rule) instead of `MarkdownView`, not alongside it — matching `UserBubble`/`AssistantTextBubble`'s own real swap ("highlighted `AttributedString` when a search query is active, else Markdown-rendered").
  - AC4 (scroll position restored on back/forward): has **no Swift equivalent at all** (confirmed directly by a dispatched research pass: no `@SceneStorage`, no stored offset/index/item-id anywhere in `SessionDetailView.swift`) — a genuine addition this port makes, not a port of existing behaviour, same category as M2-19's truncation message. Saved on `onUnmounted` (reading the virtualizer's own `scrollOffset`) and restored on `onMounted` (setting `scrollTop` directly and dispatching the same native `scroll` event a real scroll would, rather than the virtualizer's own `scrollToOffset` — see Deviations for why), keyed by session id in a Map that needs to survive the `:key`-forced remount `SessionsPage.vue` now does on every session switch (so `onMounted`/`onUnmounted` reliably bracket exactly one session's viewing, matching Swift's own `.onChange(of: session.id)` reset boundary).
  - `SessionSkeleton.vue` ports `SessionSkeletonView.swift`'s own shapes/ratios directly (header bar and badge row, 6 alternating bubble placeholders with per-index line-width ratio tables, a tool-block-shaped extra rect at index 1/3). Swift's own shimmer-sweep modifier has no direct port; Tailwind's own `animate-pulse` utility is used instead (already available, no new CSS).
  - `npx vue-tsc --noEmit` and `npx eslint .` (0 errors, the same 2 pre-existing warnings) both pass. `npx vite build` now shows `SessionsPage`'s own chunk grow from 12.19 kB to 344.52 kB (Shiki's language grammars, previously unreachable, are real weight now that `TextBlock` to `MarkdownView` is finally wired into a real screen) — expected, not a regression; port-plan §9's performance budgets are M2-28's own dedicated gate, not this task's. `npx vitest run` → **285 passed** across 28 files (up from 279 across 27 — `SessionDetail.spec.ts` is new, 6 tests).
- **Deviations:**
  - The virtualizer's own `scrollToOffset` method was the first version of the AC4 restore and never actually worked: empirically confirmed (a debug probe showed the DOM's `scrollTop` staying 0 after the call) that the virtualizer's own scroll-element wiring is itself a reactive effect that resolves one tick after `onMounted` starts, so the call raced ahead of it and silently no-opped. Fixed by setting `scrollTop` directly and dispatching the native `scroll` event the virtualizer's own offset tracking is definitely already listening on by that point (the same mechanism the *save* side, and `maybeLoadMore`'s own trigger, already use) — confirmed via virtual-core's real source (its own rect measurement reads `offsetWidth`/`offsetHeight`, not `getBoundingClientRect`, a second real finding this task's own test setup needed; and the default scroll-to function calls `element.scrollTo`, which happy-dom does honour, ruling that out as the cause).
  - A real bug in this task's own first draft, not inherited: the scroll-position cache was declared as a top-level `const` inside `SessionDetail.vue`'s `<script setup>` block on the assumption it was module-scope (like `store.ts`'s own `sessionCache`) — it is not. Every top-level binding in `<script setup>` compiles into the component's `setup()` function body and is re-created per *instance*, not per module, so each remount got its own empty Map and the restore always found nothing. Fixed by moving the declaration into a plain, non-setup `<script>` block in the same file (a documented Vue SFC pattern for exactly this: sharing a real module-level singleton alongside `<script setup>`), confirmed by the same debug probe once the fix was in place.
  - `ProjectRow.vue`/`SessionCard.vue`'s click handlers are unchanged (still call `sessionStore.selectSession` directly) — `SessionsPage.vue`'s route sync mirrors the store into the URL rather than the other way around, so no existing click path needed touching.
  - Per the stub rule, `SessionFacetsCard.vue`/`SessionTodos.vue`/`FileHistory.vue` are not wired into `SessionDetail.vue`'s own template at all, unlike M2-09's placeholders (which *were* mounted into `AppShell.vue` despite rendering nothing) — the difference is real, not a shortcut: `SessionDetail`/`LoadedSession` carries no facets or todos field, and nothing fetches either; `isShowingFileHistory` has no store state anywhere (M2-14's own documented reduction) and nothing can ever set it true without the toolbar button that toggles it (M2-22). Wiring a condition that can never be true, or a card with no data source, would be inert scaffolding, not working code with a smaller surface.
- **Follow-ups:** `SessionFacetsCard`/`SessionTodos`/`FileHistory` each need a real data-fetching task before they can be wired into `SessionDetail.vue` at all (none exists yet for any of the three). `isShowingFileHistory` needs a store home once M2-22 builds the toolbar button that sets it. The bubble action buttons (format toggle, copy-as-Markdown, copy raw text), the scroll-to-bottom FAB, and the conversation context card are not built — none are named by this task's own AC, and the first two depend on a Markdown-export helper that doesn't exist yet either. `SessionGroup`-style agent grouping remains unbuilt (M2-15's own already-documented reduction) — the timeline toggle and per-group logic in the detail-pane's state machine (session-views.md §3.3a) are not reproduced. `allBlocksExpanded`'s live-reactivity for `ToolBlock.vue` (flagged since M2-18) is still not wired — no store field exists for it yet. The scroll-position cache and `sessionCache` both grow without ever being evicted for a deleted session; negligible in practice (a handful of bytes per session) and not fixed here, the same judgement call `sessionCache` itself already made.

- **Evidence:**

This was `docs/spec/session-views.md`'s own Open Question #1, left for whichever task actually built this card; both formulas are ported verbatim here (`fraction`: `current/window`, clamped 0–1; `cacheHitRate`: `cacheReadTokens / (inputTokens + cacheReadTokens + cacheCreationTokens)`) as the one place that needs them.
  - **A second real finding:** `GaugeRing.vue` (M2-07's own "shared components" task) already has the exact ring/severity math `UsageGaugeRing`+`UsageWindow` provide (confirmed: identical 80/95 thresholds, identical green/orange/red tints) — but had no real caller anywhere in the codebase yet (`grep`-confirmed), and its `width`/`height` were hardcoded to 36, while this card needs 50 (`ConversationContextCard.swift:40`). Reused directly rather than building a second ring component, via a small, backward-compatible `size` prop addition (default 36, so nothing about `GaugeRing.spec.ts`'s own existing 10 tests — none of which assert a literal width/height — needed to change).
  - AC (the sparkline is inline SVG; spec covers the empty-series case): the sparkline is a plain `<svg>`/`<polyline>`, built from `ContextSparkline.path(in:)`'s own normalized-polyline algorithm (each point's height relative to `context.window`, ported verbatim) — never a canvas or a charting library. `ConversationContextCard.spec.ts` covers: the polyline renders with real, non-empty `points` for a multi-turn series; the whole sparkline column is omitted (not rendered empty) for `series: []`; and, a case beyond the AC's literal wording but directly adjacent to it, a single-point series also omits it (session-views.md §11's own gate is `series.count > 1`, not `> 0` — one point has no line to draw). Two further tests (beyond the bare AC) exercise the card's other real figures: the current/window/cache-hit-rate text, the token-mix bar's three segments summing to 100% of its width, and a genuinely zero-token session producing `0%`/no `NaN` rather than a divide-by-zero artifact.
  - **A real mismatch found between this task's own Goal prose and its named Input file, resolved by trusting the source:** the Goal says "the timeline lists tool calls," but the only Input file for it, `SessionTimelineView.swift`, is not a tool-call list at all — it is a Gantt chart of one session *group*'s parent and sub-agent sessions over time (`TimelineEntry`/`BarMark`, confirmed by reading the file directly). `SessionTimeline.vue` ports that real behaviour: a header with an agent-count pill, horizontal bars positioned by each entry's start/end time within the group's overall time range (plain positioned `div`s, not a chart library — ECharts' Gantt support needs its `custom`-series `renderItem` API, real setup cost for a view with no real caller and no acceptance criterion gating it at all, this task's one AC being about the context card's sparkline only), colour-coded the same way (`accent` for the parent, `blue`/`green`/`purple` for `explore`/`plan`/anything-else agent types), a legend, and a `select` emit on a bar click (`TimelineEntry`'s own click-to-navigate, session-views.md §12).

- **Deviations:**

  - `ConversationContextCard.vue` *is* wired into `SessionDetail.vue` (unlike `SessionTimeline.vue`) because it has a real, already-available data source today: `session.detail.context`, gated the same way Swift gates the card itself (`!context.isEmpty`, i.e. `series.length > 0` — a different, outer gate from the sparkline's own inner `series.length > 1` gate inside the card).
  - While investigating whether to add a spec for `SessionTimeline.vue`'s `select` emit (not required by Outputs, so ultimately not added), a real but unrelated `@vue/test-utils` behaviour surfaced: `wrapper.emitted()` did not report a `defineEmits`-declared custom event fired via `$emit` in a from-scratch minimal repro, even though the emit demonstrably reached a real parent listener (verified directly via an `onXxx` attrs callback, which did fire) — a test-introspection quirk in this project's exact Vue/test-utils versions, not a bug in this component. Not chased further: nothing in this task's Outputs or AC needed `SessionTimeline.vue`'s own spec at all, and the emit's real behaviour is independently confirmed correct.
- **Follow-ups:** `SessionTimeline.vue` needs `SessionGroup`'s own grouping logic (and a timeline-toggle piece of UI state, `showTimeline`, session-views.md §3.3a) before it can be wired into `SessionDetail.vue` for real — a session-grouping task is the natural owner, not this one. Its `select` emit has no test coverage (see Deviations) and no real caller to drive it yet. The `wrapper.emitted()` quirk found while investigating that is worth a second look if a future task actually needs to assert on a custom emit via `.emitted()` specifically — this task worked around it by not depending on that API at all, not by root-causing the test-utils behaviour itself.

Both are written to match Tauri's own documented WebDriver/CDP integration patterns as closely as this session's own research could confirm; real verification is Windows and Linux CI.
  - AC4 (`e2e/README.md` records the macOS WebDriver answer): records it directly from port-plan.md's own assumption A-10 ("tauri-driver has no macOS support because WKWebView has no WebDriver implementation at all") — a real, cited, structural platform fact, not a guess — and names the fallback precisely: no real-app smoke exists for macOS at all; the mocked WebKit run (which stands in for WKWebView per P-8's own framing) is the only coverage that OS gets.
  - `npx vue-tsc --noEmit` and `npx eslint .` (0 errors, the same 2 pre-existing warnings — a first pass left 2 harmless "unused eslint-disable directive" warnings in the new files, `--fix`ed before finishing) both pass against the new `e2e/**/*.ts` files too, confirmed covered already by this project's own `tsconfig.json`/`eslint.config.*` (both already listed/ignored the right paths in advance of this task, not something this task had to add). `npx vitest run` stays at **300 passed** across 30 files, confirming the new `e2e/` tree is correctly invisible to Vitest's own `src/**/*.{test,spec}.ts` include glob. `npx vite build` still succeeds.
- **Deviations:**
  - **A real bug in this task's own first draft, caught empirically, not inherited:** `mockIpc.ts`'s first version wrapped every handled command's return value in `{status: 'ok', data: value}` before giving it to `window.__TAURI_INTERNALS__.invoke` — the exact same mistake M2-19's own `EditDiff.spec.ts` mock made and fixed, recurring here because this file was written fresh rather than by analogy. `__TAURI_INTERNALS__.invoke` stands in for the *raw* Tauri IPC call; `typedError()` (`bindings.ts`) is the layer that wraps that raw value into `{status, data}` on the way out to callers. Double-wrapping it meant `ipc.listProjects()` resolved to the inner `{status, data}` object instead of the real payload, and `sessionStore.load()`'s own `summaries.map(...)` crashed with "Cannot read properties of undefined (reading 'map')" — caught by writing a small standalone debug script (`page.addInitScript` plus `page.on('pageerror', ...)`) against the actual built frontend, not guessed at. Fixed by returning each case's raw value directly; re-running the same debug script afterward showed real fixture session titles/previews rendering, not just an empty sidebar shell.
  - A second, smaller real bug, also caught empirically: the `webServer`'s `url: 'http://127.0.0.1:4173'` check didn't detect an already-running `vite preview` process because that process (started by an earlier, interrupted run) was listening on `[::1]:4173` (IPv6) only, while Playwright's own health check used the IPv4 loopback address — a mismatch, not an actual reachability problem. A second new `vite preview` attempt then hung trying to bind the same already-occupied port instead of failing fast. Fixed by passing `--host 127.0.0.1` explicitly to `vite preview` in `playwright.config.ts`'s own `webServer.command`, matching the URL it's checked against exactly.
  - A third, separate real bug fixed in `tauriCdp.ts` itself while exercising it directly (see AC2/AC3 evidence): a missing/unspawnable binary emits Node's `'error'` event on the child process, not `'exit'` — the first version only listened for `'exit'`, so a bad binary path crashed the whole script with an unhandled `'error'` event instead of a clean, catchable rejection. Fixed by also listening for `'error'`.
  - `e2e/mocked/app.smoke.spec.ts` is beyond this task's own literal Outputs list, for the reason given in AC1's evidence above.
- **Follow-ups:** AC2/AC3 need a real Windows and Linux CI run to actually prove (this dev machine cannot build `heimdall-desktop` at all, per the already-documented `windres` gap) — this task's own first real CI run on this branch is the next chance to find out whether `tauriCdp.ts`/`wdio.conf.mjs` work exactly as written against a real binary, the same "first real run is the first chance to find out for certain" position M1-58's own CI-dependent tasks were already in. `mockIpc.ts`'s unhandled-command fallback (`console.warn` + resolve `null`) is deliberately permissive for a broad smoke test; a future task writing a *specific* mocked scenario (not just "does it render") may want a stricter per-spec handler instead, not a change to this shared default.

### M2-24 · Visual baselines for the session screens · 2026-10-11

- **Agent:** test-automator   **Reviewers:** pending (qa-expert)
- **Status:** done
- **Files touched:** `e2e/visual/session.spec.ts` (new) — this task's own Outputs list, except its own `-snapshots/` folder (see Deviations: deliberately not committed from this machine). Plus, beyond Outputs (each real, necessary infrastructure the spec needs, not scope creep): `e2e/visual/visualData.ts` (new: tool/result/diff/thinking fixtures ported verbatim from `ScreenshotData.swift`), `e2e/support/fixtures.ts` + `e2e/support/mockIpc.ts` (modified: `get_session`/`get_session_messages`/`diff_lines` handlers and four stable "visual session" fixtures, needed because individual block screenshots require a real loaded session, which M2-23's own mock never had to answer before), `playwright.config.ts` + `e2e/README.md` (modified: a third `visual` project, WebKit-only per P-13), `src/features/session/blocks/{ToolBlock,ThinkingBlock,EditDiff}.vue` (modified: one `data-testid` each, so a spec can target one block instance precisely — Swift's own tests snapshot one `View` directly; the web has no such built-in isolation, so this is the equivalent hook), `src/features/session/SessionsPage.vue` (modified: two real, previously-undetected bugs in M2-20's own route-sync, both found by this task's own spec — see Evidence/Deviations).
- **Evidence:**
  - **A real architectural finding before writing the spec:** `ScreenshotTests_ToolBlocks.swift` (and `ThinkingBlocks`/`EditDiff`) snapshot one SwiftUI `View` directly (`ToolBlockView(tool:, result:)`), not a whole screen — confirmed by reading the real test file. The web has no equivalent "mount one component in isolation and screenshot it" primitive through a real browser page, so the faithful port is: load one real, full session into the real app (through the same mocked-IPC harness M2-23 built) and screenshot *one block instance* within it, located by a `data-testid` added to each block component for exactly this purpose — not a `VisualHarness.vue`-style standalone-component route, which would need its own routing/mounting infrastructure for no benefit over using the real screen everything else already renders through.
  - AC1 (each listed Swift suite has a matching Playwright test): all 8 suites have at least one real test. Three are ported in full against real fixture data: ThinkingBlocks (4/4), EditDiff (4/4), and a close equivalent of ToolBlocks (11/15 — every real tool collapsed, plus long-content/error/no-output/unknown-tool; the 4 missing are expanded-state variants already visually covered by the conversationView screenshots, not a gap). SessionDetail (6/8) and Sidebar (2/4) cover what's real and buildable; Standalone (2/10) captures the two pieces with a real dedicated component (`ProjectSessions`'s grid, `SessionSkeleton`); FullViews (0/9) and Windows (0/3) are honestly not represented — every one of their own sub-cases targets content that doesn't exist yet (Settings/Onboarding/Help/Config screens are still M2-09/M3-16/M3-17 stubs; multi-agent needs `SessionGroup`, M2-15's own documented gap) or, where it does exist, is the same underlying screen SessionDetail/ToolBlocks/ThinkingBlocks already cover (Hero/SessionBrowser/ConversationView/Search), not a separately-composed "full view."
  - AC2 (conversation view in 3 themes x 2 font scales): a parameterized loop over the three *adaptive* themes (`default`/`solarized`/`highContrast` — confirmed directly against `src/theme/themes.ts`'s own comment: these three are the ones that "follow `prefers-color-scheme`," the one real, principled reading of "3 themes" out of the 20 the app actually ships, since the other 17 are each single-appearance already) x two font scales (1.0, 1.5 — `FONT_SCALE_MIN`/`MAX` from `theme.ts`, the two extremes). All 6 combinations run and pass.
  - `npx vue-tsc --noEmit` and `npx eslint .` (0 errors, the same 2 pre-existing warnings) both pass. `npx vitest run` stays at **300 passed** across 30 files (this task touches no Vitest-covered source behaviour beyond the two `SessionsPage.vue` bug fixes, both re-verified green). `npx vite build` still succeeds. `npx playwright test --project=chromium --project=webkit` (M2-23's own mocked smoke) still passes — the two `SessionsPage.vue` fixes below don't regress it. `npx playwright test --project=visual --update-snapshots` → **33 passed**, run repeatedly on this machine until every failure was root-caused for real (see Deviations) — the end state, not the first attempt.
- **Deviations:**
  - **Two real, previously-undetected production bugs in `SessionsPage.vue`'s route-sync (M2-20), both found only because this task's spec drives real session selection end-to-end for the first time (M2-20's own spec never navigated by URL at all):**
    1. `selectSession()`'s first synchronous write sets `selectedProjectId` before the async `get_session` fetch resolves `selectedSession` — a real race: the "Store → route" watcher saw that split-second "projectId set, session not yet" state, read it as "navigated to a project with no session," and pushed that to the URL, stripping the session id a direct link had just asked for — which then fed back through the "Route → store" watcher and deselected the session entirely. A **direct URL to a session silently landed on the project grid instead**, confirmed with a throwaway debug spec before guessing at a fix. Fixed by skipping the "Store → route" push while `sessionStore.isLoadingSession` is true; the watcher fires again once the fetch settles, with the complete state.
    2. The "Route → store" watcher's own "project selected, no session" branch called `deselectSession()` alone, which only clears the *session* half of the selection and never sets `selectedProjectId` — so a project-only URL (`/sessions/:projectId`) never actually selected the project, and the UI fell through to the "nothing selected" empty state instead of `ProjectSessions`'s grid. Fixed by setting `sessionStore.selectedProjectId` directly in that branch before calling `deselectSession()`.
  - Several real bugs in the spec itself, also found and fixed empirically rather than guessed: `getByText('<session title>')`/`getByText('<project name>')` are inherently ambiguous in this master-detail layout (the same title/name legitimately renders twice — once in the sidebar list, once in the detail/grid header) — fixed with `getByRole('heading', {...})`, scoped to the one `<h1>` each screen actually has. All four synthetic "visual session" fixtures originally shared one literal title, a second, compounding ambiguity — fixed by giving each a distinct one. The Mod+T dispatch used `window.dispatchEvent(...)`; `useShortcuts.ts` listens on `document`, a different `EventTarget` with no bubbling between the two — fixed to `document.dispatchEvent(...)`. `testLongContentTruncated`'s own name doesn't match its ported-verbatim fixture data (72 lines, under `ToolBlock.vue`'s 80-line threshold) — the assertion was changed to match reality rather than inflating the fixture away from `ScreenshotData.swift`'s real numbers.
  - This task's own `-snapshots/` folder is **not committed**: every image this machine can generate is tagged `-win32.png` by Playwright's own platform-aware naming, and port-plan.md P-13 commits Linux baselines only — WebKitGTK's font rendering differs enough from every other engine (and OS) that a baseline captured anywhere else is the wrong answer, not a noisier one. The spec was run locally (`--update-snapshots`) purely to prove it is mechanically correct — all 33 cases pass — then the generated images were deleted before this commit.
- **Follow-ups:** The real Linux baselines still need to be generated for real, from a Linux CI runner (`--update-snapshots` on `ubuntu-latest`) and committed from there — this task proves the spec is correct, not that baselines exist yet. `e2e/visual/` is not wired into any CI workflow at all (that's M2-29's own job, "CI: frontend"). FullViews/Windows/the remaining Sidebar and Standalone cases stay unrepresented until their own underlying screens exist (Settings/Onboarding/Help/Config, `SessionGroup`, toast components, config badges) — tracked here, not silently dropped. The two `SessionsPage.vue` bugs this task found were real regressions sitting in already-merged M2-20 code with no test coverage catching either — a concrete argument for not treating a route-sync feature as "done" without exactly this kind of end-to-end exercise, for whichever future task touches session selection next.

### M2-25 · XSS corpus through renderMarkdown · 2026-10-11

- **Agent:** test-automator   **Reviewers:** pending (qa-expert, security-auditor)
- **Status:** done
- **Files touched:** `fixtures/security/xss-payloads.json` (new, 17 payloads), `tests/security/xss-corpus.spec.ts` (new, this repo's first file under the top-level `tests/` tree port-plan.md's own directory map already names), `vitest.config.ts` (modified: `include` now also matches `tests/**/*.{test,spec}.ts`, so this new directory is actually picked up — `fixtures/README.md` already named M2-25 as this fixture's owner and `tsconfig.json`'s own `include` already anticipated `tests/**/*.ts`, so only the Vitest glob itself was missing).
- **Evidence:**
  - AC1 (payloads include every listed vector): 17 payloads across 10 categories, one JSON entry per ADR 0010's own list — `script` (a literal `<script>`), `event-handler` (`onerror` on an `<img>`, `<svg onload>`), `disallowed-uri` (`javascript:`, `data:`, `file:` link destinations — 3 separate payloads), `iframe` (a remote-origin `<iframe>`), `remote-image` (a markdown image whose src would beacon on load if ever rendered as a real `<img>`), `style-attribute` (a full-viewport-overlay `style=` on an otherwise-allowed `<a>`), `meta-refresh` (`<meta http-equiv="refresh">`), `link-smuggling` (two variants: a reference-style link whose real destination sits in a separate `[ref]: javascript:...` definition line, and a link whose `title=` rather than `href=` carries the payload, probing whether sanitization only checks one attribute), `entity-obfuscation` (3 variants: decimal-entity `&#106;avascript:`, hex-entity `&#x6A;avascript:`, and plain mixed-case `JaVaScRiPt:`, each probing whether the URI allowlist check runs before/after entity decoding and whether it's case-sensitive), and `fenced-code` (the same raw `<script>`/`onerror` HTML inside a fenced block, with and without a language tag). A dedicated test (`'the fixture corpus is non-empty and covers every category ADR 0010 lists'`) asserts the category set directly against ADR 0010's own list, so a future edit that silently drops a category fails loudly rather than just shrinking the corpus quietly.
  - AC2 (assertions walk the DOM): `assertSafeDom()` parses each `renderMarkdown()` result with a real `DOMParser` (file carries `// @vitest-environment jsdom` — same reasoning as `renderMarkdown.spec.ts`: DOMPurify's own README says happy-dom, this project's default environment, isn't safe to combine it with, confirmed for real under this repo in that earlier task), then walks every element asserting: no `script`/`iframe`/`img`/`meta`/`object`/`embed`/`svg`/`style`/`link`/`base`/`form` tag anywhere, no attribute name starting with `on` or named `style` on any element, and every `href`/`src` that does survive matches the exact `/^(?:https?:|mailto:|#)/i` allowlist `sanitizeConfig.ts` itself enforces — restated independently here rather than imported, so a future change to `sanitizeConfig.ts` can't silently change what this corpus considers "safe" without this file also changing. Verified the walker is a real check, not a vacuous pass, with a throwaway temporary test (deleted before commit, per this session's own established practice) asserting it throws on a raw `<script>`, a raw `onerror=`, and a raw `javascript:` href fed to it directly outside the real pipeline — confirmed failing loudly, then removed.
  - `npx vitest run` → **320 passed** (300 before this task, +20 here: 17 per-payload tests + the category-coverage test + 2 extra targeted assertions for the remote-image and fenced-code cases specifically). `npx vue-tsc --noEmit` clean. `npx eslint .` → 0 errors, the same 2 pre-existing warnings.
- **Deviations:** `tests/security/xss-corpus.spec.ts` first used `node:fs`'s `readFileSync` to load the fixture (matching `contrast.spec.ts`'s own `// @vitest-environment node` pattern) — but this file needs `jsdom`, not `node`, for DOMPurify, and Vite externalizes `node:fs`/`node:url` as browser-incompatible under *any* DOM-shaped test environment, not just `happy-dom` (`contrast.spec.ts`'s own comment only names `happy-dom`, which turned out to be the wrong scope — it's anything non-`node`). Fixed by importing the fixture JSON directly (`import payloads from '../../fixtures/security/xss-payloads.json'`), matching `dto-fixtures.spec.ts`'s own existing convention for the same reason, and used by M2-01 already. One assertion originally expected the literal substring `&lt;script&gt;` in the fenced-code case's output; Shiki tokenizes escaped markup across several per-character/per-token `<span>`s, so the three escaped characters never appear contiguously — fixed to check for `&lt;` alone (proof the bracket was escaped at all) and rely on `assertSafeDom`'s own DOM walk, which already proves no real `<script>` element exists, for the rest.
- **Follow-ups:** `fixtures/security/xss-payloads.json`'s own flat `{id, category, description, markdown}` shape was chosen so M2-26 (malicious-transcript E2E) can place each payload's `markdown` string verbatim into user text, assistant text, tool output, thinking, a plan and a memory file without any reshaping — M2-26 is the next task to confirm that assumption holds. M3-35 (chart XSS tests) is the other named consumer of this same fixture, for ECharts' `encodeHTML` formatters rather than `renderMarkdown()` — a different rendering path than this task touches at all. `tests/security/` is now a real, Vitest-covered directory for the first time; any future top-level test file placed there needs no further config changes, this task already wired the glob.

Both consumers place their bucket's content as literally that location: the fixture tree's JSONL puts the `user` bucket in the first message's `content` string, the `assistant` bucket in the second message's `text` block, the `tool-output` bucket in that message's `tool_result` content, the `thinking` bucket in its `thinking` block, the `plan` bucket as the entirety of `plans/malicious-plan.md`, and the `memory` bucket as the entirety of `projects/.../memory/MEMORY.md` — matching `docs/spec/claude-home-inventory.md` R21/R8 exactly (global `plans/*.md`; project-scoped `memory/*.md`). The mocked session (`buildMaliciousSessionDetails`) places the same four renderable buckets (user/assistant/tool-output/thinking) into Heimdall's own DTO shape for the Playwright half.
  - AC2 (fails on any dialog, URL change, non-app-origin request, or new page; runs mocked in WebKit and against the real app on Linux and Windows): `e2e/security/malicious-transcript.spec.ts` attaches `page.on('dialog'|'popup'|'request')` before navigating, renders the full malicious session (including toggling `ToolBlock`'s "Show rendered markdown" so the tool-output bucket actually reaches `MarkdownView`'s v-html path — see Deviations), clicks the one real surviving link, and asserts all three arrays stay empty and the origin never changes. **Passes locally**: `npx playwright test --project=security` → 1 passed. `tauriCdp.ts`'s new `assertNoMaliciousTranscriptEscape` (Windows) runs the identical four checks over the real CDP connection, gated on `claudeHome.includes('malicious')`; `app.smoke.mjs`'s new, `HEIMDALL_E2E_CLAUDE_HOME`-gated `describe` block (Linux) checks window count, alert-dialog absence and origin stability — honestly not off-origin requests, since tauri-driver/WebKitGTK's WebDriver protocol has no CDP-style network interception at all (unlike Windows' real CDP connection or the mocked spec's Playwright one). Neither real-app half could be executed on this dev machine, the same already-documented `windres` gap blocking every `heimdall-desktop` build here (M2-12 onward) — written to match `tauriCdp.ts`/`wdio.conf.mjs`'s own already-established patterns as closely as possible; real verification is Windows/Linux CI, same position M2-23's own real-app smoke was already in.
  - `npx vue-tsc --noEmit` and `npx eslint .` (0 errors, the same 2 pre-existing warnings) both pass. `npx vitest run` stays at 320 passed (this task adds no Vitest specs). `npx playwright test --project=chromium --project=webkit` (M2-23's own mocked smoke) still 2/2 passed — `fixtures.ts`'s additions are purely additive.
- **Deviations:**
  - **A real discovery that changed the fixture's own tool choice:** `ToolBlock.vue`'s `showRawContent` ref starts `true` for *every* tool and there is no toggle to leave raw text at all for Bash (`v-if="!isBash()"` on the only button that flips it) — Bash output can never reach `MarkdownView` through the UI at all, by original Poirot-ported design, not a bug. The fixture's tool_use was switched from `Bash` to `Read` (both in the real JSONL tree and the mocked session) specifically so the toggle exists and this task's spec can click it, actually exercising the v-html/DOMPurify path for the tool-output bucket instead of only ever testing Vue's own safe-by-construction text interpolation.
  - **A second real discovery that changed which link the spec clicks:** `markdown-it` runs with `html: false` (confirmed already in M2-25's own work), so every *raw-HTML* payload — `onerror`, `<svg onload>`, `<iframe>`, `<meta refresh>`, and this corpus's three raw `<a href>` scheme-obfuscation variants — renders as inert escaped text before DOMPurify is ever involved; none of them produce a real, clickable element to test the click-interception layer against. `linkify: true` still auto-links the bare URL substring inside the iframe-remote payload's own escaped text, though, producing one real `<a href="https://evil.example/">` — confirmed directly via Playwright's `error-context.md` accessibility-tree snapshot on a failed run, then used deliberately as the one link this spec clicks, rather than the originally-planned (but, it turns out, never-real) `style-attribute-clickjack` link.
  - A first version of the spec computed `appOrigin` from `page.url()` before the first `page.goto()`, when the page is still `about:blank` — every request after that read as "off-origin" against that bogus baseline, and the very first test run failed on the app's own asset requests. Fixed by reading the Playwright `baseURL` fixture instead.
  - Plans/Memory have no UI at all yet (`PlansPage.vue`/`src/features/memory` are M2-06-era stubs, same gap M2-24 already documented) — their two payload buckets exist correctly in the real fixture tree (ready for whichever M3 task builds those screens) but have no live-DOM proof today, honestly narrower than the other four locations.
- **Follow-ups:** The real-app halves (Windows `tauriCdp.ts`, Linux `app.smoke.mjs`) are unverified until a real Windows/Linux CI run exercises them — this task's own best-effort selectors (clicking the fixture's one session by position, not by exact title, since the real session's Rust-derived title is unknowable ahead of time from attacker-controlled first-message text) may need adjustment once that first real run finds out for certain, the same position every other real-binary-dependent task this session has been in. Once Plans/Memory screens exist, their malicious content is already staged and ready — no fixture rework needed, just new assertions in whatever spec covers those screens. `security-auditor` review is flagged pending per this task's own named reviewers.

- **Evidence:**
  - AC1 (the scroll spec samples frame times with requestAnimationFrame on a generated 10k-block transcript and asserts the budget): `gen-large-transcript.mjs`'s `generateLargeTranscriptMessages()` builds 8,334 messages (10,000 content blocks exactly at the stated threshold: plain text on every message, plus a `Bash` tool_use/tool_result pair on every 5th assistant turn, so the virtualizer sees real row-height variance, not 10,000 identical bubbles). `transcript-scroll.spec.ts` loads that session through the mocked-IPC harness, then runs a 4-second `requestAnimationFrame` loop in-page that increments `scrollTop` by 60px per tick and records each inter-frame delta, computing median/p95 in Node and asserting against port-plan.md §9's own numbers (≤16.7ms median, ≤33ms p95). **Measured on this machine: median 16.70ms, p95 16.80ms — both PASS**, re-run twice with identical results (not a fluke) and landing almost exactly on the 60Hz vsync interval (1000/60 ≈ 16.667ms) — the signature of a scroll that isn't bottlenecked at all for this (lightweight, synthetic) content.
