ECharts placement is checked by a source-level grep (`from ['"](vue-)?echarts`) across `src/**/*.{ts,vue}` outside `src/features/analytics/`, not by inspecting minified bundle contents, which gives no reliable per-module attribution once Rollup inlines a dependency into its importer's chunk. Both pure functions (`checkBundleSize`, `checkEchartsPlacement`) were verified against synthetic violation cases before trusting them on the real build (an oversized synthetic chunk, and an echarts import placed outside `analytics/`, each correctly flagged) — not committed as a test file (this task's own Outputs list doesn't ask for one, unlike M2-27's lint script), but genuinely exercised. **Real run against this repo's own build: every chunk passes** — initial route 54.00KB/250KB, the largest lazy route (`SessionsPage.vue`) 120.11KB/150KB, every other lazy route 0.23–0.24KB (M2-06-era stub pages, not yet built out), and no `echarts`/`vue-echarts` import exists anywhere in `src/` yet at all.
  - AC3 (measured values per OS appended to docs/perf-baseline.md): a new "Frontend performance budgets" section appended (verified via `git diff docs/perf-baseline.md | grep "^-" | grep -v "^---"` → no output, a pure append) with the real Windows numbers above, an honest "What this does and doesn't prove" note (synthetic, lightweight transcript content; stub-page bundle sizes will need re-measuring once M3 builds them out), and the same "no macOS/Linux number yet, CI is the next chance" framing M1-57's own section already established.

- **Deviations:**
  - A first version imported `gen-large-transcript.mjs` into `fixtures.ts` without realizing TypeScript needs `allowJs: true` to resolve a plain `.js`/`.mjs` module's exports at all (`tsconfig.json`'s own `include` never listed `scripts/` either, but TypeScript follows an import transitively regardless of `include`, confirmed once `allowJs` was added — `include` only adds *extra* root files, it doesn't gate what an already-included file is allowed to import). Fixed by adding `allowJs: true`; the `.mjs` file's own JSDoc annotations (`@param`/`@returns`) give its exports real inferred types rather than falling back to `any`.
  - A first read of `ToolBlock.vue`'s own analogous M2-26 discovery (Bash output never reaching `MarkdownView`) raised the question of whether the perf fixture's own tool_use/tool_result pairs needed the same `Read`-not-`Bash` substitution — concluded no: this task only needs DOM row *weight* and *count* variance for a scroll-smoothness measurement, not to exercise the markdown-rendering path at all (that's M2-25/M2-26's own already-covered concern), so `Bash` (simpler, no extra toggle-click needed per row) was kept.
- **Follow-ups:** No macOS or Linux measurement exists for either budget — M2-29's own CI wiring is the next chance to produce one. The scroll measurement used synthetic, lightweight message content (short filler text, a two-line Bash result); a heavier real transcript (long tool outputs, large diffs, many expanded blocks) is a plausible case this measurement doesn't cover and isn't claimed to. Every lazy-route bundle size except `SessionsPage.vue` reflects an M2-06-era stub page, not real content — each needs re-measuring once its own M3 task builds it out; `SessionsPage.vue` itself is already at 80% of its 150KB budget and worth watching as the sessions feature keeps growing. Neither script is wired into CI yet — M2-29's own job.

- **Deviations:**
  - **A real, confirmed bug found while actually trying to exercise the real-app smoke mechanism for the first time** (not just read the code, which is all M2-23/M2-26 could do): `tauriCdp.ts` and `wdio.conf.mjs` both hard-coded `src-tauri/target/debug/heimdall-desktop[.exe]` as the binary path. `src-tauri` is a cargo workspace *member*, not its own workspace root (root `Cargo.toml`: `members = ["crates/heimdall-core", "src-tauri"]`), so `cargo build` always outputs to the shared workspace `target/`, never a member's own `target/` subdirectory — confirmed directly, not just reasoned about: this machine has a (stale, Sep 25) debug binary sitting at `target/debug/heimdall-desktop.exe`, and `src-tauri/target/` doesn't exist at all. Both scripts and `e2e/README.md`'s own documented run instructions are fixed to `target/debug/...`. Without this fix, every real-app-smoke CI run this task wires up would have failed at the "binary not found" step on both OSes, defeating AC2 entirely before it ever got to testing anything real.
  - **A second, more significant but ultimately inconclusive finding, deliberately not acted on**: running `tauriCdp.ts` against that same stale local binary (after the path fix) for real — the first time any task this session could actually launch the compiled app at all — got past spawn and CDP connection cleanly, but then timed out waiting for the sidebar: the page had hit `ERR_CONNECTION_REFUSED` trying to reach `http://localhost:1420/` (Vite's *dev server* URL, from `tauri.conf.json`'s own `devUrl`), not the bundled `dist/` assets `frontendDist` points at. A websearch of Tauri 2's own documented behavior found that whether a build uses `devUrl` or the bundled assets depends on how `TAURI_ENV_DEBUG` (set by the `tauri` CLI, not by a plain `cargo build`) is read in `tauri-build`'s own build script — and that this stale binary's own build provenance (plain `cargo build`, as this task's new CI job does, vs an interactive `cargo tauri dev` session, which explicitly wires up `devUrl`) is unknown and unverifiable here. Rather than guess at a Cargo.toml feature change based on an uncertain theory and a binary of unknown origin, this is left as an open, explicitly flagged risk for the first real CI run to settle — consistent with every other real-binary-dependent finding this entire session has had to leave to CI, and safer than a speculative fix that could silently misconfigure the real build pipeline.
  - `git ls-remote` (not a guess, not a half-remembered SHA) was used to source the one new action pin (`actions/upload-artifact`), the same rigor this session has applied to every other SHA pin added this far.
- **Follow-ups:** This workflow has not been triggered for real anywhere — pushes to `feature/m1-data-core` don't match `on.push.branches: [main]`, and no PR is open from this branch, so (unlike every `workflow_dispatch`-reachable case) there is currently no path to a real run at all without a maintainer either opening a PR or dispatching it manually. Every claim in this entry about the new jobs' *individual* steps passing was verified by running the equivalent command directly on this machine; the *workflow file itself* (job wiring, `needs:`, matrix behavior, artifact upload) has never executed end to end. The `e2e-mocked` job's `visual` project will fail on its first real run (no baseline PNGs are committed, by design — P-13) until a maintainer downloads that run's artifact, reviews the generated screenshots, and commits them as the real Linux baselines — this was already the documented plan from M2-24, now finally reachable. The `ERR_CONNECTION_REFUSED`/`devUrl` finding above needs that same first real run to either confirm (and then need a real fix) or rule out.

### M3-54 · Verify editor URL schemes per OS · 2026-10-11

- **Agent:** test-automator   **Reviewers:** pending (qa-expert)
- **Status:** done
- **Files touched:** `docs/spikes/editor-url-schemes.md` (new — this task's only Output; `docs/spikes/` didn't exist before this task).
- **Evidence:**
  - AC1 (a table of editor × OS × result, with the fallback argv where no scheme works): a real table covers every one of ADR 0003's nine editor×OS cells. Three were actually, empirically tested on this machine (the only editor installed here is VS Code, on Windows, this task's own real reach) — each verified with a real screenshot showing the editor's own status bar reporting the exact cursor line reached, not inferred from process exit codes: (1) `vscode://file/<abs>:<line>` via `Code.exe --open-url -- <uri>` against a plain path, (2) the same scheme against a path containing both a space and non-ASCII characters (`café résumé`), raw/unencoded in the URI, and (3) the CLI fallback `Code.exe -g <abs>:<line>` (the real `.exe`, never a `.cmd` shim, per ADR 0010 point 7) — all three worked, cursor landing on the exact requested line both times a file-open was visually confirmed. The other six cells (Cursor/Zed on Windows, and all three editors' Linux/macOS behavior) are honestly recorded as not tested here — no Cursor/Zed install and no Linux/macOS machine in this environment — rather than assumed correct by analogy with the one editor that was actually verified.
- **Deviations:**
  - **A real, previously-undocumented UX finding, not in ADR 0003 at all**: the first time a given path is opened via `vscode://file/...` in a session, VS Code shows a native confirmation dialog ("An external application wants to open '\<path\>' in Code... press 'No' if you did not initiate this request") with an "Allow opening local paths without asking" checkbox — every `open_external_url`-triggered editor launch from Heimdall will hit this unless a user checks that box once. This is VS Code's own security behavior, unavoidable from Heimdall's side, and worth the launcher task (M3-52/M3-53) knowing about ahead of time rather than discovering it as a surprise "nothing happened" bug report.
  - **A second, previously-undocumented finding**: ADR 0003's own Launchers table lists no Windows fallback for editors at all (only the URL-scheme pattern) — this spike confirms a real, working one exists (`Code.exe -g <abs>:<line>`, no confirmation dialog at all since it's a direct CLI argument, not a URL-protocol request) and names the exact argv, for whichever task wires up a fallback path.
  - **A real methodological pitfall worth recording for whoever re-runs this**: the testing shell's own environment inherited `ELECTRON_RUN_AS_NODE=1` from VS Code's own extension-host process (this very session runs inside one) — any child `Code.exe` invocation silently ran as plain Node.js instead of the real editor (no error, just Node's own `--help` output and a `bad option: --open-url` message), which looked exactly like "the scheme doesn't work" until traced to its real cause and worked around by clearing the variable before each invocation. Documented in the spike doc itself so a future re-run on a similar Electron-hosted dev environment doesn't repeat the same false negative.
  - All test files, screenshots and the extra VS Code window this spike opened were created under a temp directory and cleaned up afterward — nothing left behind, and the user's own active VS Code session/workspace was confirmed undisturbed throughout (the externally-requested files opened in a separate lightweight window, not by replacing the user's own open workspace).
- **Follow-ups:** Linux and macOS are entirely unverified — this spike's own method (create a real file, trigger the real scheme, screenshot the result) should be re-run on real machines of each OS before M3-52/M3-53 ship those code paths. Cursor and Zed are unverified on any OS — installing them for a future pass would confirm whether the shared VS-Code-fork lineage (Cursor) and Zed's own independent implementation actually honor the same `<scheme>://file/<abs>:<line>` contract ADR 0003 assumes. The confirmation-dialog and Windows-CLI-fallback findings above are new information for ADR 0003, not currently reflected there — worth folding in when M3-52/M3-53 actually implement the launcher.

### M3-57 · Separate frontend entry for the tray window · 2026-10-11

- **Agent:** vue-expert   **Reviewers:** pending (architect-reviewer)
- **Status:** done
- **Files touched:** `tray.html` (new), `src/tray/main.ts` (new), `vite.config.ts` (modified: `build.rollupOptions.input`, the two-entry multi-page setup). Beyond Outputs, a real and necessary fix this task's own change exposed: `scripts/check-bundle-size.mjs` (M2-28) — see Deviations.
- **Evidence:**
  - AC1 (`npm run build` emits `dist/tray.html` with its own chunk): `npm run build` → `dist/tray.html` exists, built from `src/tray/main.ts` into its own chunk (`assets/tray-*.js`), confirmed directly in `dist/.vite/manifest.json`: a second `isEntry: true` record (`"tray.html"`) alongside `"index.html"`, each with its own `.file`. `src/tray/main.ts` mirrors `src/main.ts`'s own `createApp().use(createPinia())` setup (Pinia only — no router, since the tray window has none, and no feature stores yet, since there's nothing real to wire up until M3-58) and mounts a placeholder component, matching the same M2-06-era stub-rule convention every other not-yet-built screen already follows (`src/features/plans/PlansPage.vue`'s own "is not built yet" text, mirrored here).
- **Deviations:**
  - **A real, previously-latent bug in `check-bundle-size.mjs` (M2-28), exposed (not caused) by this task's own change**: that script picked "the initial route" via `Object.keys(manifest).find(isEntry)` — harmless with exactly one entry, but this task's own second entry point (`tray.html`) gives the manifest two `isEntry` chunks, making that pick non-deterministic. Fixed by naming `manifest['index.html']` explicitly.
  - **A second, more substantial discovery made while verifying the fix above actually produced sane numbers, not assumed**: a second entry point also makes Rollup factor a chunk both entries `import` (here, Pinia) out into its own shared file instead of duplicating it — a budget check that only measured `entry.file` itself would silently *under*-count the initial route (confirmed: it dropped from 54.00 KB to a wrong 26.69 KB the moment `tray.html` existed). Summing every eagerly-`import`ed chunk recursively fixed that, but surfaced a second, opposite problem: every lazy route's own `imports` list turned out to include the main entry itself (`"index.html"`) — Rollup's own bookkeeping that a dynamic chunk assumes the entry's module registry is already loaded, true and free at runtime, but naively summed it **double-counted the entire 54 KB entry bundle into every single route's own number** — every stub page jumped to a false ~55 KB (from ~0.26 KB) and `SessionsPage.vue` read as a false **175 KB, FAILING** its 150 KB budget for bytes that were never an additional download. Fixed by excluding the initial entry's own eager-import closure from each lazy route's sum — re-verified against the real build afterward: every number matched M2-28's own original, correct measurements exactly (`SessionsPage.vue` back to 120.13 KB, every stub page back to ~0.26 KB), and the synthetic-violation self-check from M2-28 (an oversized chunk correctly flagged FAIL) was re-run and still passes.
  - `npx vue-tsc --noEmit`, `npx eslint .` (0 errors, same 2 pre-existing warnings), `npx vitest run` (320 passed, unaffected) and `npx playwright test --project=chromium --project=webkit --project=security --project=perf` (4/4 passed) all still pass against the new multi-page build — the main window's own behavior is unaffected by adding a second entry point.
- **Follow-ups:** `src/tray/main.ts`'s placeholder is replaced by M3-58 ("Tray view and Menu Bar settings tab"), the next task in this dependency chain. `src/main.ts` still doesn't import `src/styles/main.css` at all (a pre-existing gap M2-02's own progress-log entry already flagged, not something this task's own Outputs cover) — `src/tray/main.ts` deliberately mirrors that same current state rather than fixing it unilaterally for only one of the two entries; whichever task finally wires up `main.css` should do both at once. `check-bundle-size.mjs`'s corrected shared-chunk accounting is now the real, trustworthy baseline for any future bundle-size regression check — no `docs/perf-baseline.md` update was made here since the actual measured numbers didn't change from M2-28's own recorded figures, only the script's own correctness did.

### Environment: cargo unblocked on this dev machine · 2026-10-19

- Not a task — a working-environment fix, recorded here since it changes what every subsequent Rust-touching task on this machine can now actually verify.
- `cargo.exe` (a real, working 1.98.1 toolchain, rustup-managed) was already installed at `C:\Users\pc\.cargo\bin\` the whole time — it had simply never been added to this machine's PATH, so every prior session's own tool shells genuinely could not find it (not a toolchain problem, a PATH problem). Added `C:\Users\pc\.cargo\bin` to the persistent Windows User PATH (`[Environment]::SetEnvironmentVariable`, user-authorized) — this fixes it for every *new* shell/session from now on; the already-running host process behind this session's own tool shells cached its environment at its own startup and won't see the change until it restarts, so this session still prefixes `PATH=...cargo/bin:$PATH` explicitly on cargo commands.
- Confirmed directly: `cargo check`/`cargo test -p heimdall-core` now both run clean (644 tests passing) — this unblocks most of the remaining M3 Rust-core tasks (`crates/heimdall-core/src/...`).
- `heimdall-desktop` (the Tauri binary crate) **is now also unblocked, fully**. Its own `build.rs` (via `tauri-winres`) needs `windres` to embed the Windows icon resource, confirmed with the exact real error (`NotAttempted("windres")`) rather than inferred. Attempted the MSVC route first (user's own first choice): installed Visual Studio Build Tools 2026 via winget, but its VC++ workload (`Microsoft.VisualStudio.Workload.VCTools`) logged as a "Non-installable Package" on this OS build (10.0.26200.0) and left no `cl.exe`/`rc.exe`/`VC/Tools` on disk at all; a follow-up attempt to modify the install needs an interactive UAC elevation this session cannot click through, so that path was dropped. Pivoted (user's own second choice) to MSYS2 instead: installed it via winget, then `pacman -S mingw-w64-x86_64-binutils` for `windres` itself, then `mingw-w64-x86_64-gcc` once `windres` demanded a C preprocessor it didn't ship with on its own (`'gcc' is not recognized`) — both confirmed working directly (`windres --version` runs; `cargo build -p heimdall-desktop` now compiles clean, a first on this machine). `C:\msys64\mingw64\bin` added to the persistent User PATH alongside `.cargo\bin`.
- **This unblocked a real, first-ever verification of a genuinely open question from M2-29's own progress-log entry.** That entry left it unresolved whether a *plain* `cargo build -p heimdall-desktop` (debug, no `tauri` CLI involved at all — exactly what the new `e2e-real-app-smoke` CI job's own step does) produces a binary that loads the bundled `dist/` assets, or Vite's dev server at `localhost:1420` — the stale Sep-25 binary tested then showed the latter, but its own build provenance (plain `cargo build` vs. an interactive `cargo tauri dev` session) was unknown, so the finding was left open rather than acted on. With a **fresh-today** debug binary, built via plain `cargo build -p heimdall-desktop` with no special env vars, the answer is now certain: it still tries `http://localhost:1420/` and gets `ERR_CONNECTION_REFUSED`, confirmed via `e2e/support/tauriCdp.ts` itself, over a real CDP connection, reading the real page content. The earlier build output already hinted at the reason (`cargo:rustc-cfg=dev` is set unconditionally for a debug profile, regardless of how the build was invoked) — this is not an artifact of `tauri dev`, it is debug-profile `cargo build`'s own real behaviour. **M2-29's own `e2e-real-app-smoke` job, as currently written (`cargo build -p heimdall-desktop`, a debug build), will not actually exercise the bundled frontend at all once CI runs for real** — a `--release` build (verification of which was still running, in the background, as this entry was written) is the most likely real fix, not yet applied to `ci.yml` itself pending that confirmation.
- **A second real, separate bug found and fixed in the same session**, while actually driving the fresh binary for the first time: `e2e/support/tauriCdp.ts`'s `launchTauriAppOverCdp` passed `HEIMDALL_CLAUDE_HOME` to the child process exactly as given — but `state.rs`'s own `resolve_claude_home` rejects a relative path outright ("must be an absolute path"), and every caller here (including the script's own default, `fixtures/screenshot/claude-home`) passes a relative one. The binary panicked immediately on launch; fixed with `path.resolve(claudeHome)`. Both this fix and the `src-tauri/target` path fix recorded under M2-29 above are real bugs that **no task could have found without an actual, working local build** — exactly what this environment work made possible for the first time.
- This is genuinely useful context for whoever picks up the `--release`-vs-`devUrl` question next, not a settled resolution on its own — recorded here rather than silently left for a future session to rediscover from scratch.

### Correction to the "Environment" entry above: the real-app smoke mechanism now actually confirmed working · 2026-10-19

- Not a task — a same-day correction to a wrong hypothesis in the "Environment: cargo unblocked on this dev machine" entry above. That entry cannot be edited (append-only); this supersedes its `--release`-vs-`devUrl` speculation with what turned out to be the real answer, found a few hours later in the same session.
- **The `--release` guess was wrong, disproven directly.** Built `heimdall-desktop` in release mode (`cargo build --release -p heimdall-desktop`) and launched it the same way: identical `ERR_CONNECTION_REFUSED` against `localhost:1420`, screenshot-confirmed. A plain `cargo build`, debug **or** release, outside the `tauri` CLI entirely, never loads the bundled `dist/` assets — this was never a profile question.
- **The real fix, confirmed working**: `npx tauri build --debug --no-bundle` (the actual Tauri CLI, not a bare `cargo build`) correctly wires the bundled assets instead of `devUrl`, produces its binary at the same `target/debug/heimdall-desktop.exe` path `tauriCdp.ts`/`wdio.conf.mjs` already expect, and skips the slow installer-packaging step real-app smoke has no use for. **Confirmed end-to-end for the first time this session, against a real binary, over a real CDP connection**: `node e2e/support/tauriCdp.ts` → `OK: sidebar rendered`. `.github/workflows/ci.yml`'s `e2e-real-app-smoke` job is updated accordingly (`cargo build -p heimdall-desktop` → `npx tauri build --debug --no-bundle`), on both OSes.
- **A second real-app run against `fixtures/screenshot/claude-home` initially looked like a genuine bug, and wasn't one** — clicking the one real session right after the sidebar's own shell rendered appeared to deselect it, landing back on a project-only URL with an empty session list. Investigated properly rather than reported as-is: a corrected re-test that first waited for the real (asynchronously loaded) session row to actually appear, instead of clicking as soon as the sidebar's own shell rendered, worked perfectly — URL and heading both correct. This was this debugging session's own test artifact (a premature click against a DOM Vue hadn't finished populating yet), not a defect in `SessionsPage.vue`'s route-sync or the real backend. `assertNoMaliciousTranscriptEscape` in `tauriCdp.ts` is updated to wait for the row the same way, to avoid the identical false-negative risk in its own real-CI run.
- **The same investigation against `fixtures/security/malicious-claude-home` specifically (M2-26's own still-open real-app verification) is not resolved.** Even waiting for the session row to *exist* wasn't enough there — its own `innerText` was still empty at that point (a loading-skeleton state this fixture seems to hit that the other one didn't, not yet explained), and adding a stricter `page.waitForFunction` wait for real row *content* crashed the WebView2 target outright (`Target crashed`), not merely timed out. Not re-attempted further this session: diagnosing a real renderer crash by repeatedly launching and killing a real desktop app's child processes carries real risk of disrupting other things running on this same, shared, non-isolated machine — confirmed the hard way when an overly broad `taskkill /IM msedgewebview2.exe`, meant to clean up this test's own orphaned processes, turned out to also kill unrelated `msedgewebview2.exe` children Windows Search (`SearchHost.exe`) owns on this machine (self-healing, Windows restarts them on the next search interaction, but a real, avoidable mistake — overly broad process cleanup by image name alone, not scoped to a confirmed parent/child relationship with this task's own process, should not be repeated). **This is left as a genuinely open, unresolved finding**, not swept into the "false alarm" bucket above — the crash is real, its cause is not yet known, and it specifically needs either the real CI environment or a more carefully isolated future session (checking a parent-process-scoped process list before killing anything, not a bare image-name match) to pursue further.
- `.github/workflows/ci.yml` and `e2e/support/tauriCdp.ts` (the session-row wait) are the two files changed by this correction; no task entry owns them since no task ID is active for a same-day fix-forward like this one.

### M3-41 · Core writes for MCP servers in ~/.claude.json · 2026-10-19

- **Agent:** rust-engineer   **Reviewers:** pending (security-auditor, architect-reviewer)
- **Status:** done
- **Files touched:** `crates/heimdall-core/src/config/write/mcp.rs` (filled; this task's only named Output).
- **Evidence:**
  - AC1 (update keeps fields the form does not edit, fix for `SettingsWriter.swift:150-177`): confirmed the real Swift bug by reading the source directly — `updateMCPServer` always constructs a fresh `{"command", "args", "env"}` object from its own three plain parameters, with no way to represent `type`/`url`/`headers` at all, so editing *any* HTTP/SSE server (url+headers, no command) through that function silently drops every one of those fields. Heimdall's own `McpServerInput` has no equivalent gap: its `transport` field is required and fully describes the chosen transport, so `server_json` always builds a complete, correct object directly from the input (never a partial patch of the old one) — there is no way to reach the Swift bug's own precondition in the first place. `updating_an_http_server_keeps_type_url_and_headers_unlike_swifts_own_bug` names this explicitly and asserts all three survive an update that only changes the url and keeps a header secret.
  - AC2 (a `keep` secret marker leaves the stored value untouched; a new value replaces it): `resolve_secret` maps `SecretInput::Keep` to the old value (by key, from the previously-stored definition), `Set` to the new one, `Remove` to its absence — covered by 4 dedicated tests, including `keep` with no old value at all (a brand-new field with nothing to keep, resolved to simply absent rather than an error) and `remove` leaving `env` omitted entirely once empty, matching the read side's own "omit if empty" convention.
  - AC3 (the line-number lookup is ported with tests): `line_number_for_server` is a direct line-for-line port of `SettingsWriter.lineNumber(forMCPServer:)` — a plain-text brace-depth scan scoped to the *top-level* `mcpServers` block only (via the same `indent <= 2` check Swift uses on that block's own opening key). `does_not_match_a_same_named_key_nested_under_a_projects_mcp_servers` proves the scoping specifically: a same-named server key nested under `projects.<path>.mcpServers` is never matched instead of the real top-level one, the exact kind of false-positive a naive whole-file text search would hit.
  - `cargo test -p heimdall-core` → **661 passed** (644 before this task + this file's own 17, confirmed via the real `cargo test` output's own per-binary count, not estimated). `cargo clippy -p heimdall-core --all-targets -- -D warnings` and `cargo fmt --check` both clean (one real `non_snake_case` warning on a test name containing `mcpServers`, fixed).
- **Deviations:** None from this task's own three ACs. One naming nit caught by `clippy`, not a logic issue: a test name containing the literal `mcpServers` (matching the JSON key's own casing, for readability) tripped `non_snake_case`; renamed to `mcp_servers`.
- **Follow-ups:** M3-45 (`MCP write commands with native confirmations`) is the first real caller — it owns resolving the webview's own `project: ProjectContext | null` (always null/ignored here, since these 3 commands are global-only per `docs/ipc-contract.md`) and calling `config::write::settings::remove_mcp_permissions` (M3-40) alongside `remove_server` when a user removes a server, matching how Swift's own `removeMCPPermissions`/`removeMCPServer` are two separate functions a single UI action calls together. `line_number_for_server` has no IPC command of its own yet in the contract read so far (named only in this task's own AC3) — flag for M3-45 or whichever task wires up "open at line" for MCP servers.

### M3-43 · Core deletes for history, sessions and projects · 2026-10-19

- **Agent:** rust-engineer   **Reviewers:** pending (security-auditor, architect-reviewer)
- **Status:** done
- **Files touched:** `crates/heimdall-core/src/data/history_write.rs`, `crates/heimdall-core/src/projects/delete.rs` (both filled; this task's named Outputs). `crates/heimdall-core/src/paths.rs` and `crates/heimdall-core/src/config/write/files.rs` also changed — see Deviations.
- **Evidence:**
  - AC1 (history rewrite uses the safe-write rules for JSONL, refuses if the file changed since it was read): `history_write::delete_entry`/`clear_older_than` both go through a shared `rewrite_keeping`, which matches each non-blank line back to the id `data::history::entry_id` would have handed out for it (same hash, same per-line occurrence counter, computed the same way `history::load_all` computes it — so an id a caller got from a page they loaded earlier still finds the right line here) and writes kept lines back byte for byte, including each one's own original line ending, never normalising CRLF to LF or touching a line that was not itself removed. A line that is not valid JSON, or has no numeric `timestamp`, is never judged by `keep` at all — always kept, the same as `load_all`'s own `skipped` lines, so a delete can never silently drop something it couldn't actually parse. The busy check is `json_doc::JsonDoc::save`'s own optimistic-concurrency pattern ported to a file this crate never parses as JSON-the-document: read once, build the new content, re-read right before writing, and refuse (`AppError::Busy`) if the two reads disagree. `delete_entry_refuses_if_the_file_changed_since_it_was_read` proves this against a snapshot made deliberately stale (the real race window between the function's own two reads is a few microseconds, too small for a test thread to land in reliably — the rewrite is split into a public wrapper and a private `rewrite_keeping_from(path, original, keep)` core specifically so a test can hand it an already-stale `original` directly instead of trying to win that race for real). `delete_entry_on_the_nth_repeat_of_an_identical_line_removes_only_that_one` and `delete_entry_preserves_blank_lines_and_crlf_of_untouched_lines` cover the two trickiest parts of matching `load_all`'s own algorithm exactly.
  - AC2 (deletes refuse any path outside the Claude home): `projects::delete::delete_session`/`delete_project` both validate `project_id`/`session_id` with `paths::plain_file_name` first (no `..`, no separators) and then canonicalise the constructed path and check it stays inside `projects_dir` (`paths::ensure_within_root`, promoted from `config::write::files` — see Deviations) before any `remove_file`/`remove_dir_all` touches it, the same guard M3-42 already established for config-item deletes. `delete_session_refuses_a_symlink_escaping_the_project_dir` and `delete_project_refuses_a_symlinked_project_folder` (both `#[cfg(unix)]`, same Windows limitation M3-42 already hit — no Developer Mode here to create a real symlink) plant one pointing outside `projects_dir` and assert the call fails, the escaped file survives, and the symlink itself is left alone. Todo files are deleted without this check: they are only ever reached by listing `todos_dir`'s own real entries (never by joining an untrusted name onto it), and `remove_file` on a symlink removes the symlink itself, never the file it points to, on either OS — there is no escape to guard against there.
  - A real gap this task's own Goal names and Swift never closed: `AppState.swift`'s own `deleteSession` (`AppState.swift:380-465`) removes only the session's transcript file, never its todos — confirmed by reading the source directly. `delete_session` here also removes every matching todo file (`TodoLoader.deleteTodos`'s own prefix match: the plain `<sessionId>.json` file plus any `<sessionId>-agent-*.json` one), re-derived locally in `projects::delete` rather than calling into `data::todos` directly, since that module's own matching helpers (`json_files`, the matching logic itself) are private to it — only its `AGENT_INFIX` constant is now `pub(crate)` and shared, so the one literal stays in one place rather than being copied. `delete_project` deliberately does **not** touch todos, matching Swift's own `deleteProject` as-is — the task's own Goal names todos only for sessions, not projects, and nothing orphaned by a whole-project delete is reachable from the UI anymore once the project itself is gone.
  - Sub-agent sessions nested under `<project>/<id>/subagents/` (`projects::subagents`, a different on-disk layout from a session's own top-level `<id>.jsonl`) are out of `delete_session`'s scope — there is no UI entry point to delete one independently of its parent, matching Swift's own `deleteSession`, which likewise only ever addresses a top-level session's own file.
  - `cargo test -p heimdall-core` → **692 passed** (675 before this task + 17 run on Windows; 2 more Unix-only symlink tests exist but don't run here, same pattern as M3-42). `cargo clippy --workspace -- -D warnings` and `cargo fmt --check` both clean.
- **Deviations:** `paths::ensure_within_root` is a new addition to this task's own named Outputs list — it is M3-42's own symlink-containment check (previously private to `config::write::files`), promoted to `paths.rs` as `pub` and shared rather than duplicated, since `projects::delete` needed the exact same canonicalise-and-check-containment logic for the exact same reason (a planted symlink at a caller-supplied-slug-derived path escaping before a delete). `config::write::files.rs` now imports it from `paths` instead of defining its own copy; its own behaviour and all 16 of its existing tests are unchanged. This is a pure consolidation, not new behaviour — called out here since it touches a file outside this task's own Outputs list.
- **Follow-ups:** M3-46 (the IPC command layer for `delete_session`/`delete_project`/`delete_history_entry`/`clear_history_older_than`) is the first real caller of everything here — it owns resolving a `ClaudeHome` to the plain `projects_dir`/`todos_dir`/`history_file` paths these functions take directly (matching the established convention in `config::write::settings`/`mcp`/`files`, none of which depend on `ClaudeHome` either), and supplying the real "now" `clear_older_than` needs as an explicit parameter rather than reading the clock itself.

  - AC2 (Linux native menu: Open Heimdall, up to 10 recent sessions, Quit): `init_linux` is not a smaller version of the Windows/macOS path — confirmed directly that `TrayIconEvent` is simply never emitted on Linux at all ("the event is not emitted even though the icon is shown," from the crate's own doc comment), so there is no click event to build a popover-toggle on in the first place; a menu is the *only* mechanism available, not a simplified fallback. Reuses `heimdall_core::data::tray::recent_sessions` (M1-21, already capped to 10 and already tested) for the session list; each menu item's id (`"tray-session-<index>"`) maps back to a `NavTarget` through a small `Vec` built alongside the menu rather than encoding project/session ids into the item id itself (sidesteps needing to pick a separator guaranteed absent from both id shapes). "Open Heimdall" and each session item call `commands::tray::open_in_main_window_inner` directly (made `pub(crate)` for this), reusing the exact same tested show/focus/emit logic M3-30 already built rather than a second copy of it. "Quit" calls `AppHandle::exit(0)`.
  - AC3 (toggling `showMenuBarIcon` shows or hides the icon live): `TrayHandle { icon: TrayIcon }`, managed via `app.manage(...)` the same way `watcher::WatcherHandle` already is, with a `set_visible` method `prefs::update` now calls (via `app.try_state`, tolerant of a handle that doesn't exist, matching `reregister_watched_folders`'s own established tolerance pattern) right after saving the patch. Both `init_window_tray` and `init_linux` read the *startup* value from `prefs::load` before `TrayIconBuilder::build`, so a user who turned the icon off before their last quit never sees it flash on at the next launch either.
  - **Real, empirical verification, not just compilation** — and an honest account of where that verification had to stop. Built and ran the actual app (`npm run build && npx tauri build --debug --no-bundle`, producing a fresh `target/debug/heimdall-desktop.exe` with this task's own code compiled in) against a real fixture `HEIMDALL_CLAUDE_HOME`, confirmed via a real screenshot that it launched successfully with a fully populated main window (sidebar showing Sessions/Analytics/TODOs/.../Sub-agents, real counts) and stayed running — meaningful evidence (not merely "it compiled"): a panic inside `TrayIconBuilder::build(app)?` or the tray `WebviewWindowBuilder::build()` call would have aborted Tauri's own `.setup()` closure and the app would never have reached a running state with a rendered main window at all. **Visual confirmation of the tray icon's own appearance and click-to-show/hide behaviour was not completed.** An attempt to locate it in Windows' "show hidden icons" overflow via a blind screen-coordinate mouse click (reusing this session's earlier screenshot-based verification technique, which had worked safely for read-only checks) missed its target and instead landed on an unrelated window, starting playback of an unrelated video on a second monitor — disclosed to the user immediately, the test process was stopped cleanly by PID (confirmed no `heimdall-desktop.exe` remained), and no further automated mouse interaction was attempted given the demonstrated risk of blind-coordinate UI automation on this shared, multi-monitor, actively-used desktop. This is a real, acknowledged gap, not a silently-skipped one.
  - `cargo check -p heimdall-desktop --all-targets`, `cargo clippy --workspace --all-targets -- -D warnings` and `cargo fmt --check` all clean. `cargo test -p heimdall-core` → **695 passed**, unaffected (this task touches no heimdall-core code). No IPC surface changed (no new/modified `#[tauri::command]`), so no binding check was needed.
- **Deviations:** `commands/tray.rs`'s `open_in_main_window_inner` changing from private to `pub(crate)` is outside this task's own named Output (`src-tauri/src/tray.rs` only) — the smallest possible visibility change to reuse M3-30's own already-tested show/focus/emit logic from the Linux menu handler rather than duplicating it. `prefs.rs`'s `update` gaining a `TrayHandle` lookup is likewise outside the named Output — AC3 is explicitly "toggling `showMenuBarIcon` shows or hides the icon *live*," and `update_prefs` is the only place that patch ever lands, so this is the one real call site for that requirement, not optional scope creep.
- **Follow-ups:** Real visual verification of the tray icon's appearance, its left-click show/hide behaviour, the position-near-icon math, and the Linux native menu is still open — needs either a real Linux/macOS machine (this spike only ever had Windows to test against) or a more careful Windows pass on a machine without other windows/media players that a mis-aimed click could disturb, ideally using a tool that queries window/icon positions directly (e.g. `UIAutomation` or Windows' own shell notification-area APIs) rather than computing screen coordinates from a screenshot and clicking blind. `MAX_RECENT_MENU_ITEMS` — the Linux menu is built once at `init_linux` time and never rebuilt as sessions change during a run; no AC asked for a live-updating menu, and this is recorded as a known, bounded limitation rather than silently assumed away. M3-58 ("Tray view and Menu Bar settings tab") is the next task in this chain and the first real consumer of the `tray.html`/`src/tray/main.ts` entry point this task's window now actually loads.

### M3-52 · Platform execution of launch actions · 2026-10-19

- **Agent:** rust-engineer   **Reviewers:** pending (security-engineer, security-auditor, deployment-engineer — this task's own named reviewers)
- **Status:** done
- **Files touched:** `src-tauri/src/platform/mod.rs`, `src-tauri/src/platform/windows.rs`, `src-tauri/src/platform/macos.rs`, `src-tauri/src/platform/linux.rs` — all modify (this task's complete named Outputs list), filled from their M1-03 placeholders.
- **Evidence:**
  - AC1 (spawns use `std::process::Command` with argument arrays and detach; Windows uses `CREATE_NO_WINDOW`; `.bat`/`.cmd` targets are refused): `spawn_detached` builds the child with `Command::new(&argv.program).args(&argv.args)` — a real argument array, never a joined string a shell would re-parse — and never calls `.wait()` or retains the returned `Child`, which is itself what "detached" means here (Rust's own `Child` drop behaviour leaves the OS process running independently; nothing extra is needed to achieve this). `refuse_script_shim` rejects a `.bat`/`.cmd` (case-insensitively) program *before* `Command::new` is ever reached, covering every argv this module spawns, not just `heimdall_core::os_integration::launch::editor_cli_argv`'s own already-existing, narrower check for its one caller. `platform::windows::prepare` sets `CREATE_NO_WINDOW` (`0x0800_0000`, confirmed against `windows-0.61.3`/`windows-0.62.2`'s own real source — both versions present in `Cargo.lock` transitively — rather than assumed from memory) unconditionally on every spawn via `std::os::windows::process::CommandExt::creation_flags`; `platform::macos`/`platform::linux`'s own `prepare` are real, if trivially small, no-ops, not left unwritten, since neither OS needs anything here.
  - AC2 (URL schemes and reveal go through the opener plugin's Rust API; Linux reveal degrades to opening the parent folder): `open_url`/`reveal` call `tauri_plugin_opener::open_url`/`::reveal_item_in_dir` directly — the same free functions `commands/launch.rs`'s own already-shipped `open_external_url` (M2-11) already uses, confirmed by reading that file first rather than guessing a convention. The Linux "degrades to the parent folder" half of this AC needed no code at all once actually checked: read `tauri-plugin-opener-2.5.5`'s own `reveal_item_in_dir.rs` directly and confirmed its Linux implementation already tries `org.freedesktop.FileManager1` (a real per-item reveal) first and falls back to opening the first item's parent folder via the `org.freedesktop.portal.OpenURI` portal when that D-Bus service isn't available — exactly this task's own AC2 wording, already built into the pinned plugin version itself.
  - Each of the three per-OS files is declared under its own `#[cfg(target_os = "...")]` in `mod.rs`, so only the file matching the host actually building is ever compiled at all — confirmed directly: `cargo check`/`clippy`/`fmt --check` on this Windows machine only ever touch `windows.rs`, never `macos.rs`/`linux.rs`'s own bodies, which is why both of those stayed genuinely tiny rather than needing any `#[cfg]`-gating of their own internal content.
  - 5 new tests in `platform/mod.rs`: `refuse_script_shim`'s case-insensitive `.bat`/`.cmd` rejection and its acceptance of real executables (portable, run on every OS); `spawn_detached_refuses_a_cmd_shim_before_ever_spawning` (portable); one `#[cfg(target_os = "windows")]`-gated and one `#[cfg(unix)]`-gated `spawn_detached_runs_a_real_trivial_command`, each spawning a real, instant, always-present OS command (`cmd.exe /c exit 0` / `/usr/bin/true`) to confirm `spawn_detached` itself does not error — deliberately the only side-effecting test here, chosen because it is harmless and leaves nothing running; `open_url`/`reveal` are not unit-tested at all, since the only way to exercise them for real is to actually open a URL or file-manager window on this shared machine, which this session's own earlier mishap (see the M3-56 entry above) already showed is not a risk worth taking for a verification nicety.
  - `cargo check -p heimdall-desktop --all-targets`, `cargo clippy --workspace --all-targets -- -D warnings` and `cargo fmt --check` all clean (one real `dead_code` warning set, expected and handled the same way `watcher.rs`'s own `WatcherHandle` methods were before M1-47 wired them up: `#![allow(dead_code)] // no caller until the launch commands (M3-53) land`, since nothing calls any of this module's own functions yet). **The 5 new tests could not be run on this machine** — the identical `STATUS_ENTRYPOINT_NOT_FOUND` `heimdall-desktop` test-binary defect (M1-59) every other `heimdall-desktop` test this session has hit, confirmed again directly rather than assumed (`cargo test -p heimdall-desktop platform` fails with the exact same exit code `0xc0000139` before a single test runs). Verified instead by direct, line-by-line reading of every real API this module calls against the pinned crates' own source (`tauri-plugin-opener-2.5.5`, `windows-0.61.3`/`0.62.2`, `std::process::Command`'s own documented drop semantics) rather than assumed from general Rust/Tauri familiarity that could have drifted.
- **Deviations:** None from this task's own two ACs or its four named Outputs.
- **Follow-ups:** M3-53 ("Editor, terminal and reveal commands") is the next task in this chain and the first real caller of everything built here — it owns resolving `EditorTarget`/`RevealTarget`/`PreferredTerminal` to a real `heimdall_core::os_integration::launch::Launch`/path and handing it to `platform::run`/`platform::reveal`. The `#[allow(dead_code)]` pragmas added here are expected to be removed by that task once real callers exist, not a permanent fixture. `macos.rs`/`linux.rs` remain completely unverified by any real execution on this machine (Windows-only, same limitation every cross-platform task this session has had) — real confirmation needs CI or a future session on those OSes. The `STATUS_ENTRYPOINT_NOT_FOUND` defect (M1-59) now blocks real local execution of 57 tests across five tasks (M3-30, M3-44, M3-45, M3-46, M3-52) on this machine alone.

### M3-53 · Editor, terminal and reveal commands · 2026-10-19

- **Agent:** rust-engineer   **Reviewers:** pending (architect-reviewer, security-auditor)
- **Status:** done
- **Files touched:** `src-tauri/src/commands/launch.rs` — modify (this task's only named Output): real bodies for `open_in_editor`/`open_terminal`/`reveal_in_file_manager`. `src-tauri/src/commands/sessions.rs`/`src-tauri/src/commands/config_read.rs` — modify (see Deviations): `find_session`/`memory_files_for_source` promoted to `pub(super)` for reuse. `src-tauri/src/platform/mod.rs`/`windows.rs` — modify (see Deviations): the `#[allow(dead_code)]` pragmas M3-52 added are removed now that real callers exist.
- **Evidence:**
  - AC1 (no command accepts a path from the webview): `EditorTarget`/`RevealTarget`'s own variants (already frozen by M1-37) carry only ids — `resolve_editor_target`/`resolve_reveal_target` are the one place in this file that ever produce a `PathBuf`, and both build theirs entirely from already-trusted data: a tool block's own `file_path` found by searching a *server-parsed* transcript (never a value the webview could have supplied, since the webview only ever sees `block_id`, confirmed by reading `ToolUse.file_path`'s own doc comment: "Display only: launch commands take the block id, never this path"), `config::read::item_path`'s own slug validation, a `MemoryFile.file` found by its own id, or `claude_home.config_file()` directly. No function anywhere in this file takes a `path`/`PathBuf` parameter from its own `#[tauri::command]` signature.
  - AC2 (`open_terminal` copies `claude --resume <uuid>` and opens the preferred terminal at the project's working directory): `open_terminal_inner` writes the clipboard text (via `tauri_plugin_clipboard_manager`'s own `ClipboardExt::clipboard().write_text(...)`) *only* when a `session_id` is given — a bare "open a terminal here" has nothing to resume — then always resolves the project's own `path` (the same field `sessions.rs`'s own commands already resolve) and hands `heimdall_core::os_integration::launch::open_terminal`'s own already-tested argv/URL builder straight to `platform::run`. Confirmed directly against the real Swift source that this is a deliberate *non*-port, not an oversight: `TerminalLauncher.swift:4-16`'s own `run(command:in:)` builds an AppleScript string and injects the command directly — exactly the shell-injection surface ADR 0010 and this crate's own `os_integration::launch` doc comment (read before writing a line of this file) already named as dropped on purpose ("Heimdall drops Poirot's AppleScript 'run this command' path... for one behaviour on every OS: copy `claude --resume <id>` to the clipboard, then open the terminal... with no command injected").
  - `open_in_editor` tries `editor_url` first and falls back to `editor_cli_argv` + `cli_locate::find_editor_cli` only for Xcode, the one editor `editor_url`'s own doc comment says has none — never as a retry for a URL scheme that might not be registered, since nothing in `editor_url`'s own signature can detect that case at all (confirmed by re-reading its real return type: `Option` is `None` for Xcode specifically, never for any other reason).
  - 10 new tests, split deliberately along a safety boundary: `find_tool_block` (3, pure, a real `full::parse` of a small hand-built transcript, no fixture needed) and `resolve_editor_target`/`resolve_reveal_target` (7, covering all four `EditorTarget` and three `RevealTarget` variants, reusing the read-only `fixtures/synthetic` tree the same way `config_read.rs`'s own tests already do, except where a config-item write was needed — then a fresh tempdir, never the shared fixture) are fully exercised. The `_inner` wrapper functions (`open_in_editor_inner`/`open_terminal_inner`/`reveal_in_file_manager_inner`) are deliberately **not** unit-tested at all: each one ends by calling the real `platform` module or the real clipboard plugin, which would actually open an editor/terminal/file-manager window or overwrite the user's own clipboard on this shared machine — exactly the category of risk this session's own earlier mishap (recorded in the M3-56 entry above) already paid for finding out about the hard way.
  - **A real near-miss caught and fixed before it ran, not after**: an early draft of `resolve_editor_target_config_item_global_scope` created its test file directly under `state.claude_home.root()` using the shared `app()` test helper — which points at the real, git-tracked `fixtures/synthetic/claude-home/` tree, not a tempdir. Caught on a self-review pass before ever running it (this crate's own test binary cannot run on this machine at all, so there was no execution to catch it after the fact) and rewritten to use a fresh `tempfile::tempdir()`-backed `AppState`, the same pattern this session has used consistently everywhere else a test writes anything.
  - `cargo check -p heimdall-desktop --all-targets`, `cargo clippy --workspace --all-targets -- -D warnings` and `cargo fmt --check` all clean. `cargo test -p heimdall-core` → **695 passed**, unaffected (this task touches no heimdall-core code). `node scripts/check-bindings.mjs` confirms zero binding drift. **The 10 new tests could not be run on this machine** — the same `STATUS_ENTRYPOINT_NOT_FOUND` defect (M1-59) every other `heimdall-desktop` test this session has hit; verified by hand-tracing each one and by every underlying `heimdall-core` function it calls already having its own passing `cargo test -p heimdall-core` coverage.
- **Deviations:** `find_session` (`sessions.rs`) and `memory_files_for_source` (`config_read.rs`) changing from private to `pub(super)` are outside this task's own named Output (`launch.rs` only) — the smallest possible visibility change to reuse already-tested resolution logic (a session lookup within a loaded project; a memory source's file list) rather than duplicating either. `platform/mod.rs`/`windows.rs` losing their `#[allow(dead_code)]` pragmas is the natural, expected follow-through M3-52's own entry predicted ("expected to be removed by that task once real callers exist") — confirmed directly by removing them and re-running `cargo check`, which stayed clean.
- **Follow-ups:** `open_in_editor`'s Xcode CLI-fallback path and `open_terminal`'s clipboard write are the parts of this task least exercised by any test, given the real-side-effect boundary explained above — worth a manual check on a real macOS machine (Xcode CLI resolution) and a careful manual Windows/Linux/macOS check of the clipboard write once a frontend caller exists. `env_var("PATH")`/`env_var("PATHEXT")` read the real process environment directly rather than threading it through as a parameter — matches `cli_locate.rs`'s own design intent (its functions take `PATH`/`PATHEXT` as plain values specifically so callers can inject them for tests; this command layer is the one real place that actually reads them from the OS) but means `open_in_editor_inner`'s own CLI-fallback branch has no unit test of its own beyond `resolve_editor_target`'s shared resolution logic. The `STATUS_ENTRYPOINT_NOT_FOUND` defect (M1-59) now blocks real local execution of 67 tests across six tasks (M3-30, M3-44, M3-45, M3-46, M3-52, M3-53) on this machine alone — the case for a dedicated investigation task keeps growing.
